   The sound elelam is uttered by boatmen and others on the Coromandel
   coast when palling together, as savas is, by boatmen on the Hoogly:
   Some derive it from valikkiṟatu.
   1740. ēlēlaciṅkaṉ poruḷ ēḻu kaṭaliṟ pōṉālum tirumpum.
   The property of Elelasingam, though it pass over seven seas, will
   return.
   Elelasingam a wealthy merchant, the disciple of Tiruvallurar.
   1741. ēvaṟ pēy kūraiyaip piṭuṅkum.
   The excited demon will take off the thatch.
   1742. ēvā makkaḷ mūvā maruntu.
   Children who do their duty unprompted are as a life-preserving remedy.
   1743. ēvukiṟavaṉukku vāyccol, ceykiṟavaṉukkut talaiccumai.
   To the taskmaster a word, to the servant a burden on the head.
   1744. ēḻai aḻuta kaṇṇīr kūriyavāḷai okkum.
   The tears of the poor are as sharp swords.
   1745. ēḻai pākkut tiṉṉa eṭṭu vīṭu aṟiyavēṇṭumā?
   Because the poor man uses betel-nut, is it to be made known at eight
   houses?
   1746. ēḻai pēccu araṇmaṉaikku ēṟumā?
   Will the speech of the poor go up to the palace ?
   1747. ēḻaip piḷḷaikkut teyvamē tuṇai.
   God is the helper of the helpless child.
   1748. ēḻaip piḷḷaikku evarkaḷum tuṇai.
   Every one is a helper to a helpless child.
   1749. ēḻaiyaik kaṇṭāl mōḻaiyum pāyum.
   Even a beast without horns will attack the poor.
   1750. ēṟac coṉṉāl erutukkuk kōpam, iṟaṅkac coṉṉāl noṇṭikkuk kōpam.
   When told to mount, the bull is angry, when told to dismount, the lame
   man is dissatisfied.
   1751. ēṟappaṭāta marattilē eṇṇappaṭāta māṅkāy.
   Innumerable mangoes on a tree no one can climb.
   1752. ēṟa viṭṭu ēṇiyai vāṅkukiṟatā?
   What ! remove the ladder after allowing one to mount ?
   1753. ēṟiṉa kompāl iṟaṅka vēṇṭum.
   One must come down on the branch by which he ascended.
   1754. ēṟukiṟa kutiraiyilum uḻavumāṭu atika uttamam.
   A plough bull is superior to a saddle horse.
   1755. ēṟu neṟṟi cūṟutalai etirkka vantāl ākātu.
   It is a bad omen to meet one with a high forehead or curly hair.
   1756. ēṟumāṟāyp pēcukiṟatā?
   What, is it to speak at random?
   1757. ēṟum tēmal iṟaṅkum paṭartāmarai kūṭum puruvam kuṭiyaik keṭukkum.
   Ascending cutaneous spots, descending ring-worm and eyebrows contiguous
   are ominous.
   1758. ēṟkavē māmi pēykkōlam atilum koñcam mākkōlam.
   The mother-in-law is frightfully ugly already, and the flour on her
   face makes her more so.
   1759. ēṟkaṉavē varukiṟa vikkiṉaṅkaḷait taṭukkavēṇṭum.
   Approaching evils should be provided against in due time.
   1760. ēṟṟakkōlukkup piṭittāl arivāṭpiṭikku varum.
   If you measure sufficient for a well-bucket pole, it may suffice for
   the handle of a sickle.
   1761. ēṟṟappāṭṭukku etirppāṭṭā?
   Is the song of the picotta responsive?
   1762. ēṉ koḻukkaṭṭai cavukkiṭṭāy? oru kācu vellam illāmaṟcavukkiṭṭēṉ !
   Cake! why so insipid? Because I lack a cash-worth of sugar.
   1763. aiṅkalak kappiyil naḻuviṉa kappi ivaṉ.
   He is a broken grain fallen out of five kalams.
   One of a large number of insignificant persons.
   1764. aiṅkātam pōṉālum akappē araikkācu kiṭaiyātē.
   Though you may go fifty miles, you will not fetch half a cash.
   1765. aiṅkātam pōṉālum, akappai araikkācu.
   Although it may go fifty miles, an agappai will fetch but half a cash.
   1766. aiṅkātam pōṉālum aṟimukam vēṇṭum.
   Though one goes fifty miles off, an acquaintance is needed.
   1767. aiṅkātam pōṉālum, taṉ pāvam taṉṉōṭē.
   Though he may go fifty miles, his own sin will still cleave to him.
   1768. aiṅkāyam iṭṭa kāram iṭṭālum taṉ nāṟṟam pōkātāmpēyccuraikkāykku.
   Though cooked with five ingredients, the wild gourd will not lose its
   odour.
   1769. aitatu nel, aṭarnta tu cuṟṟam.
   In rice plants, distance is required, in kindred, closeness.
   1770. aintu ciṭṭukku iraṇṭu kācu vilai.
   The price of five chits a small bird is two cash.
   1771. aippaci mātam aḻukait tūṟal kārttikai mātam kaṉa maḻai.
   In October drizzling, in November heavy rain.
   1772. aippaci mātattu veyyalil aṉṟu uritta tōl aṉṟu kāyum.
   In the sunshine of October a skin dries the day it is stript off.
   1773. aippacikkum kārttikaikkum maḻai illāviṭṭāl aṇṇaṉukkum cari
   tampikkum cari.
   If the rains of October and November fail, the elder and younger
   brother will be on a par.
   1774. aippaci mātattu erumaikkaṭāvum mārkaḻi mātattu nampiyāṉum cari.
   A buffalo in the month of October and a Vaishnava brahman in December
   are alike.
   1775. aimpatilē aṟivu, aṟupatilē aṭakkam, aṟupatukku mēl oṉṟumillai.
   At fifty discrimination, at sixty moderation, after sixty no
   distinguishing characteristic.
   1776. aimpatu vayatāṉavaṉukku aintu vayatup peṇṇā ?
   Is a girl of five fit to be the wife to a man of fifty ?
   1777. aiyaṅkārum tottuk koṭuppār.
   Even the Aiyangar may communicate contagious disease.
   1778. aiyappaṭṭāl paiya naṭa.
   If in doubt, advance slowly.
   1779. aiyappaṉ kutiraiyai vaiyāḷi viṭṭa katai.
   Like the story of one who rode Aiyanar's horse.
   1780. aiyamāṉa kāriyattaic ceyyal ākātu.
   To do a doubtful thing is bad.
   1781. aiyam ēṟṟum aṟivē ōtu.
   Though reduced to beggary, learn to be wise.
   1782. aiyar eṉpavar tuyyar āvar.
   T'hose who are entitled to be called brahmans are holy.
   1783. aiyar varukiṟavaraiyil amāvāciyai niṟkumā ?
   Will the new moon await the brahman's arrival ?
   1784. aiyar katir pōla ammāḷ kutir pōla.
   The husband is like an ear of corn, the wife is like a rice bin or
   grain receptacle.
   1785. aiyaṉār paṭaiyiṟ kuyavaṉār paṭṭatu pōla.
   As the potter perished in the army of Aiyanar.
   1786 aiyaṉār kōvil ceṅkal attaṉaiyum teyvam.
   The bricks of Aiyanar's temple are so many gods.
   1787. aiyaṉār kōvil maṇṇai mitittavar attaṉaiyum piṭāri.
   Those who have trodden the ground of Aiyanar's temple are so many
   Pidaris.
   1788. aiyaṉārē vārum kaṭāvaik kaikkoḷḷum.
   Come, Aiyanar, and accept a goat, a sacrifice.
   1789. aiyaṉ amaippai ārālum taḷḷakkūṭātu.
   No one may dispute the pre-ordination of god.
   1790. aiyō eṉṟāl ivaṉukku āṟu mācattup pāvam cuṟṟum.
   The man who expresses commiseration for him will incur six months sin.
   1791. okkap piṟanta taṅkai ōlamiṭṭu aḻaccē oppārit taṅkaikkuc
   ciṟṟāṭaiyām?
   When one's sister is weeping for a cloth, is it to be given to a woman
   who resembles her?
   1792. occiyam illāta ūrilē peṇ vāṅkiṉa katai.
   The story of a man taking a girl to wife in a village inhabited by the
   shameless.
   1793. oṭinta kōl āṉālum ūṉṟukōl irukkavēṇṭum.
   Though a broken one, a walking stick is necessary.
   1794. oṭṭikkoṇṭu vantum taṭṭik kaḻikkiṟāṉ.
   Though I cling to him, he repels me.
   1795. oṭṭiṉāl toṭṭilum koḷḷum oṭṭāviṭṭāl kaṭṭilum koḷḷātu.
   If compressed the crib will hold it, but if not even a bedstead will
   not contain it.
   1796. oṭṭiṉālum uḻakkuppīrc ceṉkiṟatā?
   Is it to say draw one ulak of milk though the animal is lean ?
   1797. oṭṭaikkup paḷuvu ēṟṟukiṟatu pōla.
   As a camel is loaded.
   1798. oti peruttut tūṇāmā?
   Though the odina tree grow large, will it do for a pillar ?
   1799. oti peruttāl uralāmā?
   Though the odina tree grow large, will it serve for a mortar ?
   1800. oti peruttu eṉṉa, upakāram illātavaṉ vāḻntu eṉṉa ?
   What avails the growth of an odina tree? Of what use is the prosperity
   of the ungenerous ?
   1801. otiyamaram tūṇāmō? ōṭṭāṅkiḷiñcil kācāmō?
   Will an odina tree do for a pillar? will a broken oyster-shell pass as
   a coin?
   1802. otiyamaramum oru camayattukku utavum.
   Even an odina tree may prove useful on an emergency.
   1803. oyyārak koṇṭaiyām tāḻampūvām uḷḷē mēyumām īrum pēṉum.
   Her tresses are graceful and ornamented with the flowers of the screw
   pine, but nits and lice breed therein.
   1804. oru uṟaiyilē iraṇṭu kattiyā?
   Are two swords contained in one sheath?
   1805. oru ūrukku oru vaḻiyā ?
   Is there but one way to a village?
   1806. oru kaṭṭu vaikkōlait taṇṇīril pōṭṭu eṭṭu āḷ kūṭi iḻuttāṟpōla.
   As a bundle of straw that had been dipped in water was dragged along by
   eight persons.
   1807. oru kampattil iraṇṭu āṉai kaṭṭukiṟatā?
   What, to tie two elephants to one pole?
   1808. oru kācu akappaṭukiṟatu kutiraik kompu.
   To earn one cash is as difficult as to find a horse with horns.
   1809. oru kācu pēṇiṉ iru kācu tēṟum.
   One cash saved is two cash got.
   1810. orukāṟ ceytavaṉ irukāṟ ceyvāṉ.
   He who has done a thing once, may do it again.
   1811. oru kācu koṭātavaṉ oru varākaṉ koṭuppāṉā?
   Will he who refuses to give acash, give a pagoda?
   A Pagoda is a gold coin worth about seven shillings.
   1812. oru kācu eṉṟa iṭattil aḻukiṟāṉ.
   He weeps when the word money is uttered.
   1813 orukuṭam pālukku oru tuḷi purai.
   Onedrop of butter-milk to a pot of milk.
   1814. oru kuṇṭilē kōṭṭai piṭikkalāmā?
   Cana fort be taken with one ball ?
   1815. oru kuḷattiṟ piṟanta tāmaraiyum alliyum orē taṉmaiyuṭaiyatalla
   atu pōla oru tāyiṉ vayiṟṟil piṟantacakōtarar iruppārkaḷ.
   The lotus flowers of a tank are not all of the same species, so the
   children of the same mother are not all alike.
   1816.oru kūṭu muṭaintavaṉ oṉpatu kūṭu muṭaivāṉ.
   He who has plaţted one basket may plat nine.
   1817. oru kūṭai kallum teyvamāṉāl, kumpiṭukiṟatu entakkallai ?
   If all the stones in a basket be gods, which shall I worship?
   1818. oru kai taṭṭiṉāl ōcai eḻumpumā?
   Can clapping be effected by one hand?
   1819. oru kai taṭṭiṉāl ōcai eḻumpumā, irukai taṭṭiṉāl ōcai eḻumpumā?
   Can clapping be affected with one, or with both hands?
   1820. oru kai muḻam pōṭumā ?
   Can cubits be measured with only one arm ?
   1821. oru kōpam vantu kiṇaṟṟil viḻuntāl āyiram cantōṣam vantālum
   eḻumpalāmā?
   When one has fallen into a well, in a fit of anger, will a thousand
   joyous considerations help him out ?
   1822. oru caṭṭiyilē iraṇṭu tailam.
   Two kinds of oil in one chatty.
   1823. oru cantippāṉai nāy aṟiyātu.
   A dog does not know the vessels used on fast-days.
   Separate vessels are kept for special occasions, and also for different
   purposes on ordinary days, as for boiling milk &c.
   1824. oru cāṇ kāṭṭilē oru muḻattaṭi veṭṭalāmā?
   Can a stick a cubit long be cut in a grove a span high?
   1825. oru taram viḻuntāl teriyātā ?
   Having fallen once are you not wiser ?
   1826. orutalai vaḻakku nūlilum cevvai .
   An ex parte statement is straighter than a line.
   1827. oru talaikku iraṇṭu ākkiṉaiyā?
   Are there two punishments for one head?
   1828. oru tāy aṟṟa piḷḷaikku ūrellām tāy.
   The whole village is mother to the motherless.
   1829. oru naṉṟi ceytavarai uḷḷa aḷavum niṉai.
   Think of those who have done you even one favour as long as you live.
   1830. oru nāṭ kūttukkut talaiyaic ciraittatu pōl.
   Like shaving the head for a single day's dance.
   1831. oru nāḷaikku ikaḻcci oru nāḷaikkup pukaḻcci.
   One day blame, another day praise.
   1832. oru nāḷaikku iṟakkiṟatu kōṭi piṟakkiṟatu kōṭi.
   Ten millions are born, and ten millions die, daily.
   1833. oru nāḷum cirikkātavaṉ tirunāḷilē cirittāṉ, tirunāḷum
   veṟunāḷāyiṟṟu.
   A man who had never laughed before, laughed on a festival day,
   consequently it became a common day.
   1834. oru nāḷākilum tirunāḷ.
   Although only one day, it is a festival day.
   1835. oru paṉai iraṇṭu pāḷai oṉṟu nuṅku oṉṟu kaḷḷu.
   One palmyra has two spathes, one yields fruit, the other toddy.
   1836. oru pāṉaic cōṟṟukku oṉṟē mātiri.
   One grain suffices to test a whole pot of boiled rice.
   1837. oru piḷḷai peṟṟavaḷukku uṟiyilē cōṟu, nālu piḷḷai
   peṟṟavaḷukku naṭutteruvilē cōṟu.
   The rice for a woman who has borne one child, is on the swinging tray,
   that of the woman who has borne four, is in the middle of the street.
   1838. oru piḷḷaiyeṉṟu ūṭṭi vaḷarttāḷām, atu ceriyāk kuṇam piṭittuc
   cettatām.
   It is said that having only one child she fed it well, but that it died
   of indigestion.
   1839. oru puttiraṉ āṉālum kuru puttiraṉ āvāṉā?
   Although he is an only son, will he become an obedient disciple?
   1840. oru poruḷ ākilum eḻuti aṟi.
   Though only one item, note it.
   1841. oru marattup paḻamā orumikka?
   What! did one tree yield all this fruit ?
   1842. oru marattup paṭṭai oru marattilē oṭṭumā?
   Will the bark of one tree stick to another ?
   1843. oru marattuk kompu oru marattil oṭṭātu.
   The branch of one tree will not stick to another.
   1844. oru maṉappaṭu, ōtuvārkku utavu.
   Besingle-minded, assist those that teach the Vedas.
   1845. oru miḷakukku āṟṟaik kaṭṭi iṟaitta ceṭṭi.
   A merchant who dammed up and drained a river to recover a grain of
   pepper.
   1846. oru miḷakum nālu uppum pōtum.
   Onegrainof pepper and four grains of salt will suffice.
   1847. oru muḻukkilē maṇ eṭukkiṟatā ?
   Whạt ! is it to take up the soil by diving once?
   1848.orumaippāṭu illāta kuṭi orumikkak keṭum.
   A family divided against itself will perish together.
   1849. oruvaṉ aṟintāl ulakam aṟiyum.
   If known to one, the world may know it.
   1850. oruvaṉ aṟinta rakaciyam ulakattil paravum.
   A secret known to one may spread through the world.
   1851. oruvaṉukku iruvar tuṇai oruvaṉai aṟiya iruvar vēṉṭum.
   Two men may help one, two are necessary that one may be known.
   1852. oruvarum aṟiyāta uccitanāmaṉ.
   One of illustrious name unknown to any.
   1853.oruvaṉaik koṉṟavaṉ uṭaṉē cāvāṉ. pala pēraik koṉṟavaṉ paṭṭam
   āḷuvāṉ.
   He who, kills one person immediately suffers death, hewho kills many is
   rewarded with a crown.
   1854. oruvaṉāyp piṟappatu oru piṟappāmā, oṉṟi maram tōppāmā?
   Is it worth being to be an only offspring ? Is a single tree a tope a
   grove ?
   1855.oruvaṉ talaiyil māṇikkam, irukkiṟateṉṟu veṭṭalāmā?
   May you cut-off a man's head because there is a ruby in it?
   1856. oruvaṉāyp piṟantāl taṉimai, iruvarāyp piṟantār pakaimai.
   When only one is born there is loneliness, when two enmity.
   1857. oruvar aṟintāl irakaciyam iruvar aṟintāl parāciyam.
   If known to one only it is a secret, if to two it is public.
   1858. oruvaṉaip paṟṟi ōrakattiru.
   Attach yourself to one person, and dwell in one house.
   1859. oru viral noṭi iṭātu.
   A single finger cannot snap.
   1860. ovvāk kūṭṭilum taṉimai aḻaku, ovvāp pēccu vacaiyōṭu okkum.
   Solitude is preferable to disagreeable society, an unpleasant
   expression may be felt as a reproach.
   1861. oḻukāta vīṭu uḷḷaṅkai attaṉai pōtum.
   If only as large as the palm of the hand a house that does not leak
   will suffice.
   1862. oḻukkam uyar kulattil naṉṟu.
   Virtue is superior to rank.
   1863. oḻukku vīṭṭilē veḷḷam vantatu pōl.
   As a flood came into a leaky house.
   1864. oḻukkukku vaitta caṭṭipōla.
   As a chatty placed under a leak.
   1865. oḻuṅku oru paṇam caḷukku mukkāṟ paṇam.
   Propriety is worth a fanam, pride, three fourths of a fanam.
   1866. oḷikkum cēvakaṉukku mukattil ēṉ mīcai?
   Of what use is a mustache to a sneaking soldier?
   1867. oḷikkap pōyum talaiyāri vīṭṭilā?
   When trying to conceal one's self, is it fit to do so in the house of
   the chief ?
   1868. oḷikkap pōyum iṭam iṭaiñcalā?
   Is the place too narrowfor one who has gone to hide himself?
   1869. oḷḷiyar teḷḷiyar āyiṉum ūḻviṉai paiya nuḻaintu viṭum.
   However enlightened and clear-headed one may be, his destiny will
   quietly creep in.
   1870. oṟṟiyum cītaṉamum paṟṟi āḷavēṇṭum.
   Property received under mortgage, or as a dowry, must be taken into
   immediate possession.
   1871. oṉṟa vanta pēy ūraik keṭuttatu pōl.
   As the demon that came for shelter destroyed the village.
   1872.oṉṟa vanta kākam uram peṟṟāṟpōla.
   As if a crow that came for shelter was strengthened in his position.
   1873. oṉṟa vanta piṭāri ūrp piṭāriyai ōṭṭiṉatu pōl.
   As a demoness that came for shelter drove away the demoness of the
   village.
   1874. oṉṟa vanta piṭāri ūrp piṭāri āṉatupōla.
   As a demoness that came for shelter became the demoness of the village.
   1875. oṉṟāṉa pirapu uṟaṅkik kiṭaikkaiyil piccaikku
   vantavaṉtattiyōtaṉattiṟku aḻukiṟāṉām.
   It is said that whilst a peerless nobleman was lying hungry and
   exhausted, a beggar cries for rice and curds.
   1876. oṉṟāl oṉṟu kuṟai illai, muṉṉālē kaṭṭat tuṇi illai.
   Not a single want, yet no cloth to put on.
   1877. oṉṟu niṉaikka oṉṟāyiṟṟu.
   One thing meditated, another effected.
   1878. oṉṟum aṟiyāḷām kaṉṉi avaḷaip piṭittatām caṉṉi.
   It is said that the virgin is innocent, and is seized with lock-jaw.
   1879. oṉṟu terintavaṉukku ellām teriyātu.
   He who knows one thing does not know all things.
   1880. oṉṟum illaiyeṉṟu ūtiṉāṉ atu tāṉum illaiyeṉṟu koṭṭiṉāṉ.
   He blew a conch to report that there was nothing; and beat a drum to
   intimate that there was not even that.
   1881. oṉṟum aṟṟavaṉukku oṉpatu peṇṭāṭṭi.
   Nine wives to him who has nothing.
   1882. oṉṟum aṟṟa taṅkāḷukku oṉpatu nāḷ caṭṭaṅkaḷ?
   Is a ceremony of nine days to be performed for a destitute woman?
   1883. oṉṟē kutirai oṉṟē rāvuttaṉ.
   One horse, one horseman.
   1884. oṉṟē rājā oṉṟē kutirai.
   One king, one horse.
   1885. oṉṟaip peṟṟāl naṉṟē peṟavēṇṭum.
   If one thing only be obtained, it ought to be good.
   1886. oṉṟaip piṭittāl cātikka vēṇṭum.
   The thing asserted must be maintained.
   1887. oṉṟaip peṟṟālum kaṭukkap peṟu.
   Though you get only one thing, get it quickly.
   1888. oṉṟaip peṟṟālum kaṉṟaip peṟu.
   If you get only one as your hire, take a calf.
   1889. oṉṟait toṭiṉum naṉṟait toṭu.
   Though you touch but one thing, touch what is good.
   Most likely referring to the choice of a wife.
   1890. ō koṭuppāṉukku vāḻkkaip paṭṭu ōṭṭam oḻiya, naṭai illai.
   Having become the wife of the wretch, I am compelled to run instead of
   walking.
   1891. ōṅkil aṟiyum uyar kaṭaliṉ āḻam, pāṅki aṟivāḷ taṉ pattāviṉ
   valimai.
   An onkil fish knows the depth of the ocean, a wife knows her husband's
   strength.
   1892. ōcai kāṭṭip pūcai cey.
   Ring the bell, and perform puja.
   1893. ocai peṟum veṇkalam, ōcaipeṟā maṭkalam.
   Sounding brass, a soundless earthen vessel.
   1894. ōṭam viṭṭu iṟaṅkiṉāl ōṭakkāraṉukku oru coṭṭu.
   On landing, a cuff for the boatman.
   1895. ōṭam viṭṭa āṟum aṭi cuṭum.
   Even a navigable river may burn the feet when dry.
   1896. ōṭam kaṭṭiṉa tūlam.
   A block of wood to which a boat is moored.
   1897. ōṭam kaṭantāl ōṭakkāraṉukku oru coṭṭu.
   After crossing, a cuff for the boatman.
   1898. ōṭam vaṇṭiyilum vaṇṭi ōṭattilum kāṇavumpaṭum.
   A boat may be sesen in a cart, and a cart in a boat.
   1899. ōṭam viṭṭa iṭam aṭi cuṭum aṭi cuṭṭa iṭam ōṭam viṭappaṭum.
   The foot may be burnt in a navigable river, and a boat may float where
   the foot was burnt.
   1900. ōṭavum māṭṭāṉ piṭikkavum māṭṭāṉ, ōyāmal pēcuvāṉ.
   He can neither run nor overtake, but he can talk continually.
   1901. ōṭavum māṭṭēṉ piṭikkavum māṭṭēṉ.
   I can neither run nor catch.
   1902. ōṭi oru kōṭi tēṭuvatilum iruntu oru kācu tēṭuvatu nalam.
   It is better to acquire a cash in quiet than a lak by running about.
   1903. ōṭi oṉpatu paṇam campātippatilum uṭkāntiruntu oru paṇam
   campātippatu naṉṟu.
   Better earn one fanam where you are, thannine fanams by running hither
   and thither.
   1904. ōṭi ōṭi uḷḷaṅkālum veḷuttatu.
   The sole of the foot has become white by constant running.
   1905. ōṭippōṉa puruṣaṉ vantu kūṭikkoṇṭāṉ, uṭaimaimēl uṭaimai pōṭṭu
   miṉukkik koṇṭāḷ.
   The husband that ran away has returned and is reconciled, therefore she
   has adorned herself with jewels to excess.
   1906. ōṭippōṉa ūril ātarittavaṉ kavuṇṭaṉ.
   He who entertained the man that fled from his own village was a
   Koundan-a man of that tribe.
   1907. ōṭippōkiṟavaṉ pāṭip pōkiṟāṉ.
   He who is running away, does so singing.
   1908. ōṭiya muyal periya muyal allavō?
   The hare that ran away was a large one, was it not ?
   1909. ōṭiyam ākilum ūṭuruvak kēḷ.
   Though it is obscene, hear it out.
   1910. ōṭiyum kiḻavikkup piṟakēyā?
   Having set out to run will it do for him to be behind an old Woman?
   1911. ōṭukiṟa pāmpaip piṭikkiṟa paruvam.
   Old enough to seize a running snake.
   1912. ōṭukiṟavaṉaik kaṇṭāl turattukiṟavaṉukku ilēcu.
   Seeing him who retreats, makes the efforts of his pursuer easy.
   1913. ōṭukiṟa veḷḷam aṇaiyil niṟkumā?
   Will a rolling flood stay at the anicut?
   1914. ōṭṭaic caṭṭi āṉālum koḻukkaṭṭai ventāṟ cari.
   The chatty may be cracked, what matters that if it bakes the cakes.
   1915. ōṭṭai nāḻikkup pūṇ kaṭṭiṉatu pōla.
   Like adorning a cracked measure with a metal rim.
   1916. ōṭṭaip pāṉaiyilē carkkarai irukkum.
   A cracked pot will hold sugar.
   1917. ōṭṭaip pāṉaic carkkarai kacakkumā?
   Will sugar, because put in a cracked pot, taste bitter?
   1918. ōṭṭai maṇi āṉālum ōcai nīṅkumā ?
   Though the bell may be cracked will it be void of sound ?
   1919. ōṇāṉ viḻuṅkiya katai pōla.
   Like the story relating to the swallowing of a bloodsucker.
   1920. ōtātārkku illai uṇarvoṭu oḻukkam.
   The unlettered have neither good sense nor virtue.
   1921. ōtuvaṉ ellām uḻuvāṉ talaikkaṭaiyil.
   All poets may be found at the entrance of the ploughman's house.
   1922. ōtuvāṉukku ūrum uḻuvāṉukku nilamum illaiyā?
   Has the poet no country, has the ploughman no land?
   1923. ōtuvārkku utavu.
   Assist instructors-the brahmans.
   1924. ōnti vēlikku iḻukkiṉṟatu, tavaḷai taṇṇīrukku iḻukkiṉṟatu.
   The blood-sucker draws its prey to a hedge, the frog to water.
   1925. ōmapiṇṭattai nāy iccittāṟpōlē.
   As if a dog longed for consecrated food.
   Spoken by Sita to Ravana.
   1926. ōyā maḻaiyum oḻiyāk kāṟṟum.
   Continual rain, and ceaseless wind.
   1927. ōyvilā nēcamē yōlamē caraṇam.
   Thou, the object my unceasing love, take me under thy protection.
   1928. ōram coṉṉavaṉ ārukku āvāṉ?
   Will any employ a person who is given to one-sided statements ?
   1929. ōram coṉṉavaṉ kuṭittaṉam pōlē.
   Like the family of him who makes partial statements.
   1930. ōr āṇṭi pacittirukka ulakam ellām kiṟukiṟeṉṟu cuṟṟukiṟatā?
   Is the whole world so giddy through famine as to allow a religious
   mendicant to suffer from hunger?
   1931. ōr āṟu tāṇṭa māṭṭātavaṉ oṉpatu āṟu tāṇṭuvāṉā?
   Will he who cannot cross one river, cross nine?
   1932. ōr ūrukkup pēccu maṟṟōr ūrukku ēccu.
   That which is polite in one country may be abusive in another.
   1933. ōr ūrukku oru pēriṭṭuk koḷḷalāmā?
   May one assume a different name in every village?
   1934. oḷavai collukku accam illai.
   No fear about Avvai's sayings.
   1935. oḷavaiyārmēlum kuṟṟam uṇṭu aṇṇāviyārmēlum paḻu tu uṇṭu. Even
   Avvai may be charged with blame, a religious teacher also may have his
   defects.
   1936. kaṅkaiyilē muḷaittālum pēyccurai nalla curai āmā ?
   Will a wild gourd become a good gourd by growing on the Ganges ?
   1937. kaṅkaiyil mūḻkiṉālum kākkai aṉṉam ākumā?
   Will a crow become a swan by bathing in the Ganges?
   1938. kaṅkaiyil āṭiṉālum, kaṇamum viṭāmaṟ ceyta pāvam tīrātu.
   Though you wash in the Ganges, sin repeated again and again will not be
   expiated.
   1939. kaṅkaiyil āṭiṉālum pāvam tīrumā?
   Will sin be expiated by bathing in the Ganges ?
   1940. kaṅkaiyilē paṭintālum pēyccuraikkāy nalla curaikkāy ākātu.
   Though a wild gourd be dipped in the Ganges, its inferiority will
   remain.
   1941. kaṅkaiyilē piṟanta nattai cālakkirāmam ākātu.
   A snail of the Ganges is not a Shalgram.
   The Shalgram is a flinty stone, containing the impression of one or
   more ammonitae, supposed by the Hindus to represent Vishnu.
   1942. kacakki mōralāmā?
   Can the fragrance of a flower be appreciated after bruising it?
   1943. kacaṭarukku illaik kaṟṟōr uṟavu.
   The base do not enjoy the friendship of the learned.
   1944. kacaṭaṟak kallārkku icai uṟal illai.
   Persons of imperfect learning have no reputation.
   1945. kacaṭāṉa kalviyiṉum kalviyīṉam nalam.
   Ignorance is better than imperfect knowledge.
   1946. kacintu vantavaṉ kaṇṇait tuṭai.
   Wipe off the tears of him who comes weeping.
   1947. kaccaṟ karuvāṭu mōṭcattiṟkup pōṉālum, piccaikkārar mōṭcattiṟkup
   pōkamāṭṭārkaḷ.
   Though dried fish may attain final emancipation-heaven-beggars cannot.
   1948. kañci varatappā eṉṟāl, eṅkē varatappā eṉkiṟāṉ.
   If I say, O Varathappa give me some kanji, he replies, O Varathappa
   where.
   1949. kaṭalilē ēṟṟam pōṭṭa katai.
   The story relating to a picotta on the sea-side.
   1950. kaṭalil iṭṭa peruṅkāyam pōla.
   Like assafoetida cast into the ocean.
   1951. kaṭalilē turumpu kiṭantālum maṉatilē oru col kiṭavātu.
   A rush may remain in the sea, but a secret will not remain in the mind.
   1952. kaṭalil karaitta puḷipōla.
   Like tamarind acid dissolved in the sea.
   1953. kaṭalait tūrttum kāriyam muṭikka vēṇṭum.
   You must accomplish your undertaking, though you my have to fill up the
   ocean.
   1954. kaṭalait tūrttālum kāriyam muṭiyātu.
   Though the sea be filled up, the thing cannot be effected.
   1955, kaṭal kotittāl viḷāva nīr eṅkē ?
   Should the sea boil, whence water to cool it?
   1956. kaṭal nīr niṟaintu āvatu eṉṉa, kāñcirai paḻuttu āvatu eṉṉa ?
   What benefit arises from the water of the ocean, what good comes
   from the ripening of the kanjirai, strychnos nux vomica, fruit ?
   1957. kaṭal tāṇṭa ācai uṇṭu, kālvāy tāṇṭak kāl illai.
   He wishes to cross the ocean, but has no feet to cross a small drain.
   1958. kaṭal mīṉukku nuḷaiyaṉ iṭṭatē caṭṭam.
   As regards the fish of the sea, whatever name the fisherman givesis
   final.
   1959. kaṭal vaṟṟik karuvāṭu tiṉṉalāmeṉṟu uṭal vaṟṟic cettatām kokku.
   It is said that the stork died while waiting for the ocean to dry, in
   the hope of getting a supply of dried fish.
   1960. kaṭal perukiṉāl karaiyum perukumā?
   Will the shore extend when the sea flows?
   1961. kaṭal perukiṉāl karai ētu?
   What avails the shore when the sea flows ?
   1962. kaṭaṟ karait tāḻaṅkāy kīḻ toṅki eṉṉa mēl toṅki eṉṉa ?
   What matters it whether the wild pine fruit on the sea-shore hangs high
   or low?
   1963. kaṭaṉōṭu kaṭaṉ kantap poṭi kāṟpaṇam.
   Though my debt be increased, let me have a quarter of a fanam worth
   more of fragrant powder.
   1964. kaṭaṉ illāta kañci kāl vayiṟu.
   One fourth supply of kanji for the stomach is better than debt.
   1965. kaṭaṉkāraṉukkuk kaṭaṉum, uṭaṉ piṟantāṉukkup paṅkum
   koṭukka vēṇṭum.
   One must pay one's creditor and give to a relation the portion due to
   him.
   1966. kaṭaṉ vāṅkik kaṭaṉ koṭuttavaṉum keṭṭāṉ ; maram ēṟik kai
   viṭṭavaṉum keṭṭāṉ .
   He who borrowed to lend was ruined; and he who let go his hold of the
   tree he had climbed also perished.
   1967. kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyum paṭṭiṉi kalyāṇam paṇṇiyum canniyāci .
   Famished though he has borrowed, an ascetic though he is wedded.
   1968. kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyum paṭṭiṉiyā!
   What to famish after having borrowed money!
   1969. kaṭaṉkāraṉukkuk kaṭaṉum paḻikāraṉukkup paḻiyum koṭukka vēṇṭum.
   Debt to the creditor and retaliation to the wrong-doer are due.
   1970. kaṭaṉkāraṉai vaikkak kaḻu uṇṭā ?
   Is there a stake for impaling debtors ?
   1971. kaṭaṉ paṭṭār neñcam pōṟ kalaṅkiṉāṉ.
   He was as disquieted as the mind of a debtor.
   1972. kaṭā kaṭā eṉṟālum maruntukku oru pīr eṉkiṟāṉ.
   Though informed that it is a he-goat, he persists in asking a drop of
   milk for a medicinal purpose.
   1973. kaṭā piṉvāṅkukiṟatu pāyccalukku aṭaiyāḷam.
   The drawing back of the he-goat shows that he is about to butt.
   1974. kaṭā mēykkiṟavaṉ aṟivāṉō koḻup pōṉaviṭam.
   Does the cowherd know where the ploughshare has passed?
   1975. kaṭāviṉ cantiṟ pullait tiṉkiṟatu pōla.
   Like eating grass screened behind a bull.
   1976. kaṭāvum kaṭāvum caṇṭai pōṭukiṟapōtu uṇṇi nacuṅkiṉāṟpōla.
   As the tick was crushed to death when the goats fought.
   1977. kaṭikōlilē kaṭṭiṉa nāy.
   A dog tied to a stick.
   1978. kaṭikka oru elumpum illai, kātil miṉukka ōlaiyum illai .
   She has not even a single bone to pick, nor has she an ear-ornament to
   polish.
   1979. kaṭikkiṟa nākam kalantu uṟavākumā?
   Will the cobra be affected by friendly intercourse?
   1980. kaṭikkiṟa nāykkuk kaḻuttil kuṟuṅ kayiṟu.
   A short rope round the neck of a bitingdog.
   1981. kaṭitāṉa piḷḷai peṟṟārukku utavumā ?
   Will an obdurate child prove helpful to its parents ?
   1982. kaṭitāṉa col aṭiyilum valitu.
   A harsh word is more painful than a blow.
   1983. kaṭitta pākkum koṭāta ciṟṟappaṉ kaṭaitteru varaiyil
   vaḻiviṭṭāṉām.
   It is said that his uncle who would not give even a bite of an
   areca-nut, bore him company to the bazaar.
   1984. kaṭitta vāy tuṭaittāṟpōla.
   Like wiping the mouth after biting.
   1985. kaṭitta pāmpukku pāl vārttāl viṣattait tarum atu
   pōlappollātavarkkuc ceyyum upakāram irukkum.
   When milk is placed before a biting. snake, in return it gives poison,
   and such is the return for the favours done to the wicked.
   1986. kaṭitta mūṭṭaiyum cari, kaṭiyāta mūṭṭaiyum cari.
   Bugs are all the same whether they bite or not.
   1987. kaṭitta nāykkuk kāṭiyaik koṭu.
   Give vinegar to the dog that bit you.
   1988. kaṭittālum kaṭikkaṭṭum. nī kollātiru.
   No matter if it bite you, do not kill it.
   1989. kaṭitta nāyaip payittiyam koṇṭatu eṉṟu colvārkaḷ.
   They will call that a mad dog which has bitten some one.
   1990. kaṭinta collilum kaṉinta collē naṉmai.
   A kind word is better than a harsh one.
   1991. kaṭiyāta mūṭṭaiyeṉṟu viṭṭu viṭuvārkaḷā?
   Will they let a bug escape because it did not bite ?
   1992. kaṭiyum cuṟukkuttāṉ aṭiyum cuṟukkuttāṉ.
   Both the bite and the stroke were quickly affected.
   1993. kaṭukiccu muṭukiccu vaṭukacci kaliyāṇam.
   The Vaduga woman's wedding is come nigh.
   1994. kaṭuku pōkiṟa iṭattil taṭi eṭuttukkoṇṭu tirivāṉ, pūcaṉikkāy
   pōkiṟa iṭam teriyātu.
   He walks about with a staff in a place that will admit a mustard seed,
   and yet he is ignorant of the place where a pumpkin might easily pass.
   1995. kaṭuku ciṟuttālum kāram pōkātu.
   Though small, the mustard seed is not without pungency.
   1996. kaṭuku pōṉa iṭam ārāyvār pūcaṉikkāy pōṉa iṭam teriyātu.
   They search for a grain of mustard seed, but not for a missing pumpkin.
   1997. kaṭuku pōṉa iṭam ārāyvār miḷaku pōṉa iṭam ārāyār.
   They search for a grain of mustard seed. but not for a grain of pepper.
   1998. kaṭukuk kaḷavum kaḷavutāṉ karppūrak kaḷavum kaḷavutāṉ.
   Theft is theft whether as regards a mustard seed or camphor.
   1999.kaṭukkāykku ataṇi nañcu, cukkiṟkup puṟaṇi nañcu.
   The kernel of the gall-nut and the skin of ginger are poisonous.
   2000. kaṭuṅkāṟṟu maḻai kāṭṭum, kaṭuñciṉēkam pakai kāṭṭum.
   Strong wind foretells rain, excessive friendship foreshows hatred.
   2001.kaṭuñ ciṉēkam kaṇṇukkup pakai.
   Excessive intimacy will prove unfriendly to one's eyes.
   2002.kaṭuñceṭṭuk kāriyakkēṭām.
   It is said that hard dealing fails in its object.
   2003. kaṭuñceṭṭuk kaṇṇaik keṭukkum.
   Unfair dealing destroys the eyes.
   2004. kaṭuñcoṟ kēṭṭāl kātukku aruvaruppu.
   It is shocking to the ears to hear harsh words.
   2005. kaṭumuṭukkaṭā cēvakā kamparici aṭā campaḷam.
   Peon, my fellow, you are too strict, you have kamboo rice only as
   wages.
   2006. kaṭumpaci kal matil uṭaittum kaḷavu ceyyac collum.
   Extreme hunger will induce a man to break through a stone wall and
   steal.
   2007. kaṭai keṭṭa vāḻvu talai kaṭṭa nēram illai.
   Labouring in menial offices she has not leisure to put up her tresses.
   2008. kaṭaikkuk kaṭai āḷ iruppārkaḷ.
   Each bazaar will have its attendant.
   2009. kaṭaicic cōṟṟukku mōrum, kāl māṭṭiṟkup pāyum vēṇṭum.
   Buttermilk for the last distribution of rice, and a mat for the foot of
   the bed are indispensable.
   2010. kaṭait tēṅkāy eṭuttu vaḻip piḷḷaiyārukku uṭaittāṟpōl.
   Like taking up a cocoanut in the bazaar and breaking it to Ganesa.
   2011. kaṭaiyilē koṇṭu maṉaiyilē vaikkiṟāṉ.
   He buys in the bazaar, and bestows at home.
   2012. kaṭaiyil arici kañcikku utavumā, apicāri puruṣaṉ āpat tukku
   utavuvāṉā?
   Will the rice in the bazaar serve for kanji, will the paramour of an
   adulteress be of service to her in distress?
   2013. kaṭaiyil vantatum ariciyō naṭaiyil ventatum cātamō?
   Is that which comes to the bazaar rice? Is boiled rice cooked by the
   way desirable ?
   2014. kaṭaiyilē kaṭṭit tūkkiṉālum aḻukaṟ pūcaṇikkāy aḻukalē.
   Although rotten pumpkins are tied up in the bazaar, they are still
   rotten.
   2015. kaṭṭappāraiyai viḻuṅkic cukkuk kaṣāyam kuṭittatupōla.
   Like drinking a decoction of dry ginger after swallowing a crow-bar.
   2016. kaṭṭāntarai aṭṭaipōla.
   Like a wood-louse on dry ground.
   2017. kaṭṭikkoṇṭu varaccoṉṉāl veṭṭikkoṇṭu varuvāṉ.
   When told to tie and bring, he cuts and brings.
   2018. kaṭṭi aḻukiṟapōtu kaiyum tuḻāvukiṟatē.
   When she embraces and weeps, her hand is groping to steal.
   2019. kaṭṭil uḷḷa iṭattil piḷḷai peṟṟuc cukkuk kaṇṭa iṭattil
   kāyamtiṉpāḷ.
   She brings forth her child where she sees a bedstead, and takes the
   prescribed tonic where she sees dry ginger.
   2020. kaṭṭi vaḻi viṭṭāl veṭṭi aracu āḷalām.
   If he organize an army and lead it, he may cut down the enemy and reign
   as a king.
   2021. kaṭṭi aḻukaiyilē eṉ makaḷē uṉakkup peṭṭiyilē kai eṉṉa ?
   My daughter, why is your hand in the basket while you embrace and weep?
   2022. kaṭṭi aṭittāl eṉṉa viṭṭu aṭittāl eṉṉa?
   It makes no difference whether you flog one bound or unbound.
   2023. kaṭṭiṉa vīṭṭukkup paḻutu colluvār palar.
   Many will find fault with a house newly built.
   2024. kaṭṭiṉa vīṭṭukkuk karuttuc colluvār palar.
   Many will express an opinion respecting a house just built.
   2025. kaṭṭiṉavaṉukku oru vīṭāṉāl, kaṭṭātavaṉukkup pala vīṭu.
   He who has built a house has only one, whereas he who has notbuilt
   makes use of many.
   2026. kaṭṭivaitta pūṉaiyai aviḻttu viṭṭu vāpūs vāpūs eṉṟāl varumā?
   On untyingacat and calling puss, puss, will it come?
   2027. kaṭṭilait tiruppip pōṭṭāl talaivali pōmā?
   Will head-achego by turning the bedstead?
   2028. kaṭṭivaitta paṇattait taṭṭip paṟittāṟpōla.
   As if one displaced and carried away money carefully tied up.
   2029. kaṭṭi vitai veṭṭi vitai.
   Provide seed-corn, and sow having tilled the ground
   2030. kaṭṭu aṟinta nāyum alla, kaṉam aṟinta kapparaiyum alla.
   He is not a dog accustomed to restraint, nor a mendicant's dish that
   knows what self-respect is.
   2031. kaṭṭukkuk kaṭṭu māṟik kaṭṭa vēṇṭum.
   Every time you have to tie, you must tie it in a different way.
   2032. kaṭṭuppaṭṭālum, kavarimāṉ mayirāl kaṭṭup paṭavēṇṭum.
   kuṭṭuppaṭṭālum mōtirak kaiyāl kuṭṭuppaṭa vēṇṭum.
   When bound, it should be by the hair of the roe, and if cuffed, it
   should be bya hand ornamented with jewels.
   2033. kaṭṭuccōṟṟu mūṭṭaiyum iḷam piḷḷaiyum eṭuppatu varuttam.
   It is difficult to carry, besides a child, boiled rice, tied up for all
   journey.
   2034. kaṭṭaikkup pōkumpōtu kālāḻi pīliyā?
   Are toe-rings necessary when a woman goes out to get(?) the firewood?
   2035. kaṭṭōṭē pōṉāl kaṉattōṭē varalām.
   If one sets out auspicious!y, he may return with honour.
   2036. kaṇakkatikārattaip piḷakkum kōṭāli.
   He is an axe splitting the tree of arithmetic.
   2037. kaṇakkaṉ kaṇakku aṟivāṉ, taṉ kaṇakkait tāṉ aṟiyāṉ.
   The accountant is clever at numbers, but he is ignorant of his own
   accounts.
   2038. kaṇakkuk kuñcaiyum kākkaik kuñcaiyum kaṇṭa iṭattilē kaṇṇaik
   kuttu.
   Bore through the eyes of a young kurnum and a young crow wherever you
   find them.
   2039. kaṇakkaip pārttāl piṇakku varum.
   When accounts are examined, difficulties arise.
   2040. kaṇakkaṉukkup paṭṭiṉi uṭaṉ piṟappu.
   To suffer hunger is natural to an accountant.
   2041. kaṇakku aṟinta piḷḷai vīṭṭil iruntāl vaḻakku aṟātu.
   There will be constant disputes in a house should one of its inmates be
   a skillful accountant.
   2042. kaṇakkaṉ vīṭṭuk kaliyāṇam viḷakkeṇṇeykkuk kēṭu.
   A marriage ceremony in the house of an accountant is a waste of oil.
   2043. kaṇakkappiḷḷai ellām eḻuttuppiḷḷai alla.
   All accountants are not learned.
   2044. kaṇapati pūcai kai mēlē payaṉ.
   The pooja of Ganesa has immediate effect.
   2045. kaṇavaṉaip pirintum ayal vīṭṭil irukkiṟatā ?
   What ! in a neighbour's house, separated from your husband?
   2046. kaṇukkāl peruttāl kaṇavaṉait tiṉpāḷ.
   If her ankle grow big, she will be deprived of her husband.
   2047. kaṇ kaṇṭatu kai ceyyum.
   What the eye has seen the hand may do.
   2048. kaṇkuttippāmpupōlap pārttiruntēṉ.
   I was waiting like an eye-snake.
   2049. kaṇ kuruṭṭukku maruntu iṭṭāl teḷiyumā?
   Is blindness curable by an external application ?
   2050. kaṇ kuruṭu āṉālum nittiraiyil kuṟaiyā?
   Though blind, does he sleep the less?
   2051. kaṇ keṭṭa piṉpu cūriya namaskāram ceytu eṉṉa palaṉ?
   What benefit can you expect from the worship of the sun if you have
   lost your eyesight?
   2052. kaṇṭa pāvaṉaiyāyk koṇṭai muṭikkiṟatu.
   Tying one's lock of hair in imitation of others.
   2053. kaṇṭa kaṇṭa kōyil ellām kai eṭuttuk kumpiṭṭēṉ.
   I worshipped with raised hands in every temple I visited.
   2054. kaṇṭatu pāmpu kaṭittatu māṅkoṭṭai.
   That which was seen was a snake, that which bit was the stone of a
   mango fruit.
   2055. kaṇṭatum kāṭci peṟṟatu pēṟu.
   Whatever is seen is a sight, whatever is received is a gift.
   2056. kaṇṭataik kēḷāviṭṭāl uṇṭu uṟaṅka māṭṭāṉ.
   He will neither eat nor sleep without asking whatever he sees.
   2057. kaṇṭataik kēḷāviṭṭāl koṇṭavaṉ aṭippāṉ.
   If she does not attend to the affairs of her family, her husband will
   beat her.
   2058. kaṇṭaṟiyātavaṉ peṇṭu paṭaittāl kāṭu mēṭu ellām vara iḻuttut
   tirivāṉām.
   It is said that if an inexperienced man marries, he will wander about
   dragging his wife; through jungles and over bills.
   2059. kaṇṭataik kaṟkap paṇṭitaṉ āvāṉ.
   By learning what he sees, a person becomes a pundit.
   2060. kaṇṭatu kēṭṭatu collātē kāṭṭumarattilē nillātē.
   Never utter what you have seen and heard, nor stand under a wild tree.
   2061. kaṇṭatu pāmpu kaṭittatu karukku maṭṭai.
   That which he saw was a snake, but he was bit by the rough points of
   the palmyra stem.
   2062. kaṇṭataik kaṟṟukkoṇṭu karaiyēṟu.
   Acquire what you can, and go ashore.
   2063. kaṇṭavaṉ eṭāṉā?
   Will he who finds, not take up?
   2064. kaṇṭāl teriyātā kampaḷiyāṭṭu mayir?
   May not sheep-hair be known as soon as seen?
   2065. kaṇṭāl muṟai collukiṟatu kāṇāviṭṭāl peyar collukiṟatā?
   If present, do you call mentioning the relationship of the party, and
   when absent, his name only ?
   2066. kaṇṭāl āyam kāṇā viṭṭāl māyam.
   If seen, duty, if not seen, fraud.
   2067. kaṇṭu cetta piṇam āṉāl cuṭu kāṭṭiṟku vaḻi teriyum.
   If one die under human observation, his corpse will find its wayto the
   place of cremation.
   2068. kaṇṭu pēcak kāriyam irukkiṟatu mukattil viḻikka veṭkamāy
   irukkiṟatu.
   I have something to speak of in person, but I am ashamed to look at his
   face.
   2069. kaṇṭum kāṇavillai, kēṭṭum kēṭkavillaiyeṉṟu irukka vēṇṭum.
   Behave as though seeing, you see not, and hearing, you hear not.
   2070. kaṇṭu eṭuttavaṉ koṭuppāṉā?
   Will be who finds and takes a thing give it up?
   2071. kaṇṇāl kaṇṭataṟkuc cāṭciyum vēṇṭumā?
   Is a witness needed to prove that which one has seen with his own eye?
   2072. kaṇṇāṟ kaṇṭatu poy akappaikkuṟi mey.
   That which one has seen is false, the sign of agappai is true.
   2073. kaṇṇāṟ kaṇṭataṟku ēṉ akappaikkuṟi?
   Why the sign of an agappai in proof of that which one has seen?
   2074. kaṇṇārak kāṇātatu mūṉṟil oru paṅku.
   That one has not seen with his own eye amounts to one third
   2075. kaṇṇālē cīvaṉ kaṭakaṭa eṉṟu pōṉālum vaṇṇāṉ kaḻutai cumantē
   tīravēṇṭum.
   The ass of a washerman must carry the pack through, though its life may
   depart with a rattling noise through the eyes.
   2076. kaṇṇāṉa pērkaḷai maṇṇākkukiṟāṉ.
   He treats as common earth those who are precious as the eyes.
   2077. kaṇṇāṉa maṉataip puṇṇākkukiṟāṉ.
   Ho wounds the heart which is as tender as the eye.
   2078. kaṇṇālē cīvaṉ kaṭakaṭaveṉṟu pōṉālum vaṇṇāṉukku maḻai nañcu.
   Though the life of the washerman is forced through his eyes by reason
   of drought, rain to him would be poison.
   2079.kaṇṇāṭi niḻaliṟ kaṇṭa paṇam kaṭaṉukku utavumā?
   Will the money reflected in a mirror avail for the payment of debt?
   2080. kaṇṇāṉa pērkaḷaip puṇṇākkiṉāy.
   You have wounded your dearest friends.
   2081. kaṇṇimai kainoṭi aḷavē māttirai.
   The twinkling of the eye, or the snapping of the fingers, is the
   measure of a moment of time.
   2082. kaṇṇiyil akappaṭṭa māṉpōl kalaṅkukiṟāṉ.
   He is dismayed like a deer caught in a snare.
   2083. kaṇṇiyil akappaṭṭa karikkuruvipōl kalaṅkukiṟāṉ.
   He is agitated as an ensnared blackbird.
   2084. kaṇṇiṟ paṭṭāṟ karikkumā, puruvattiṟ paṭṭāṟ karikkumā?
   When will it give pain, when it hits the eye or the eye-brow?
   2085. kaṇṇiṉāl eṇṇuvāṉ.
   He can count with his eyes— at sight.
   2086. kaṇṇiṟ puṇ vantāl kaṇṇāṭi pārkkal ākātu.
   When the eyes become sore, one cannot see them reflected in a mirror.
   2087. kaṇṇiṉ muṉṉē oṉṟu collik kāṇāta iṭattilē iraṇṭucollukiṟatu.
   To assert a thing when in sight and to contradict it when out of sight.
   2088. kaṇṇilē eṇṇey paṭṭāṟ karikkumō piṭariyil paṭṭāl
   karikkumō?
   Will oil give pain when it falls into the eye, or on the back of the
   neck?
   2089. kaṇṇiṟ paṭṭa kaiyait taṟippār illai.
   No one cuts off the hand because it has struck the eye.
   2090. kaṇṇilē paṭṭāl viralait taṟikkiṟatā ?
   Should it strikethe eye is the finger to be cut off?
   2091. kaṇṇilē varukiṟatu puruvattilē.
   The eye brow has received that which threatened the eye.
   2092. kaṇṇil kaṇṭatu kōṭi kāṇātatu aṉantam kōṭi.
   That which was seen was a crore, and that not seen many crores.
   2093. kaṇ uḷḷapōtē kāṭci.
   One has the pleasure of seeing as long as the eyes are unimpaired.
   2094. kaṇ ūṉaṉ kaipporuḷ iḻappāṉ.
   The blind will lose his wealth.
   2095. kaṇṇukkup puruvam kātāmā?
   Is the brow ten miles in advance of the eye?
   2096. kaṇṇukkup puṇṇum alla, kāṇpārkku nōyum alla.
   It is neither an eye-sore, nor painful to beholders.
   2097. kaṇṇum karuttum uḷḷa pōtē kāṇōm ataṉ piṉpu eṉṉa kiṭaikkum?
   We do not realize it when the eye and mind are unimpaired ; what may we
   expect to gain afterwards ?
   2098. kaṇṇukku imai kātāmā?
   Is the eye-lid far apart from the eye?
   2099. kaṇṇukkum cammaṇam koṭṭuvāḷ kampattil aintāṉai kaṭṭuvāḷ.
   She will sit in one's eye cross-legged, and tether five elephants to
   the pole of a dancer.
   2100. kaṇṇai imai kāttatupōl.
   As the eye-lash preserved the eye.
   2101. kaṇṇaik koṭutta teyvam matiyaik koṭuttatu.
   The deity that deprived him of sight gave him superior mental
   endowments.
   2102. kaṇṇaik koṇṭu naṭantatu pōla uṉṉaik koṇṭu naṭantēṉ.
   I guarded you as I did my own eyes.
   2103. kaṇṇaik kaṭṭik kāṭṭil viṭṭatu pōla.
   As one was left in the jungle blindfolded.
   2104. kaṇṇaik kāṭṭi aḻaittāl varātavaḷ kaiyaip piṭittu iḻuttāl

   Will she who would not come when called by the expression of your eyes,
   obey when taken by the hand?
   2105. kaṇṇaik keṭutta teyvam kōlaik koṭuttatu.
   The deity that destroyed the eyes gave a supporting staff.
   2106. kaṇṇai mūṭik kuṭṭukiṟatā?
   Is it to strike the head after covering the eyes?
   2107. kaṇṇō puṇṇō.
   Is it an eye, or a sore?
   2108. kaṇ terintu naṭappavarkaḷ paḷḷattil viḻamāṭṭārkaḷ.
   They who walk with their eyes open will not fall into the pit.
   2109. kaṇterintu vaḻi naṭakkumpaṭi niṉai.
   Take care that you walk with your eyes open.
   2110. kaṇ pārttāl kai ceyyum.
   If the eye have seen it, the hand may perform it.
   2111. katavaic cāttiṉāl nilai puṟampu.
   When the door is shut, the door-frame is outside.
   2112. katiravaṉ cilaraik kāyēṉ eṉkumō?

   2113. katiraik kaḷaintum kaḷaiyaip piṭuṅku.
   Weed the corn even at the risk of destroying the ears.
   2114. katir nūl kuṟaintālum kaḷḷacci kaḻuttu nūl kuṟaiyātu.
   Though the thread on the spindle diminishes, that on the neck of a

   2115. kataikkuk kālum illai talaiyum illai.
   Mere rumours have neither head nor foot.
   2116. kataikkuk kāl illai pēykkup pātam illai.
   Rumours have no legs, nor have demons feet.
   2117. kataiyō pirāmaṇā kantaiyō pottukiṟāy, allaṭī pēy muṇṭāy cīlaippēṉ
   kuttukiṟēṉ.
   Obrahman, do you repeat a story or mend old clothes? No, youfoolish

   2118. kattarikkāy cottai eṉṟāl arivāḷ maṇaik kuṟṟam eṉkiṟāḷ.
   When it is said that the brinjal is worm eaten, she imputes it to a
   defect in the knife.
   2119. kattarikkāyilē kālum kaiyum muḷaittāṟpōla.

   2120. kattarikkāy virai curaikkāy kāykkātu.
   The seed of a brinjal will not produce gourds.
   2121. kattarikkāy vāṅkap pūcaṇikkāy kocuṟā?
   When you buy a brinjal, will the bazaar people give you a pumpkin in at

   2122.kattiyum kaṭāvum pōlē.
   Like a knife and a he-goat.
   2123. kattiyaip pārkkilum kaṉa kōpam kolai ceyyum.

   2124. kattikaṭṭi peṇcāti eppōtum kaimpeṇcāti.

   2125. katti irukkum iṭattukku marai kāvukiṟatā?

   2126. kattumaṭṭum kattippōṭṭuk katavaic cāttiviṭṭup pō.

   When repeatedly urged to bray, even an ass will not do so, when asked

   2128. kanta poṭik kaṭaikkāraṉukku vācaṉai teriyumā?

   2129. kantaikkut takka pontai.

   2131. kantai āṉālum kacakki uṭu, kūḻ āṉālum kuḷittuk kuṭi.

   2132. kapaṭa iṉ colliṉum kaṭiya collē nalam.

   2133. kapālak kuttu kaṇṇaic cuḻittatu.

   2134. kappalilē pātippākkup pōṭṭatu pōlē.
   Like dropping a bit of areca-nut in a ship.
   2135. kappal ēṟip paṭṭa kaṭaṉ koṭṭai nūṟṟut tīrkkavēṇṭum.
   Debt incurred by the sea-faring merchant must be discharged by spinning

   2136. kappal ōṭip paṭṭa kaṭaṉ koṭṭai nūṟṟut tīrumō?
   Can a debt incurred on account of a sea-faring life be discharged by

   2137. kappaṟkāraṉ peṇṭāṭṭi toppaikkāri, kappal uṭaintāl piccaikkāri.
   The ship owner's wife is in good condition as long as the ship is safe,

   2138. kappaṟkāraṉ vāḻvu kāṟṟu aṭittāl pōccu.

   2139. kamariṟ kaviḻnta pāl amararkku iṭṭa aṉṉam.

   2140. kampap piccaiyō kaṭaip piccaiyō?

   2141. kampaḷiyilē ēṟṟacōṟṟai mayir mayir eṉkiṟatā ?

   2143. kampaḷiyil oṭṭiṉa kūḻaippōla.

   2144. kampaḷimēl piciṉ.
   Gum on a cumbly.
   2145. kampaṉ vīṭṭuk kaṭṭuttaṟiyum kavi pāṭum.

   Arrange it so that when he goes to weed the rye, he may also engage a

   2147. kammāḷaṉ pacuvaik kātu aṟuttuk koḷḷavēṇṭum.

   2148. kammāḷaṉ vīṭṭil piḷḷai piṟantāl tēvaṭiyāḷ teruvil carkkarai

   2149. kammāḷaṉ nāy cammaṭṭit toṉikku añcumā?

   2150. kayiṟu illāp pamparam pōla.
   Like a top without a string.
   2151. kayiṟṟaip pāmpeṉṟu eṇṇik kalaṅkukiṟatu.

   2153. karaṭi piṟai kaṇṭatu pōla.

   2157. karaṇam tappiṉāl maraṇam.

   2161. karu illāta vittaiyum kuru illā cīṣaṉum pōl.
   Like an art without ingenuity to comprehend it, and a disciple without

   2162. karukki urukki neyvārttālum kaṇṭa niyāyantāṉ colluvāṉ.

   2163. karuṅkaṇṇi paṭṭāl karaiyāṉ kaṇṇālum tirumpip pārāṉ.

   2164. karuṅkallilē nār urippāṉ.

   2165. karuṅkāli ulakkaikku veḷḷip pūṇ kaṭṭiṉatupōla.

   2167. karuṭaṉ kālil keccai kaṭṭiṉatu pōla.

   2169. karuppaṅkaṭṭiyilum kallu irukkum.

   2170. karum oḻuṅku perumaikku aḷavu.

   2171. karumattai muṭikkiṟavaṉ arumai oṉṟum pārāṉ.

   2172. karumāṉ irunta iṭamum kaḻutai puraṇṭa kaḷamum cari.

   2173. karumpilum tēṉ irukkum kaḷḷiyilum pāl irukkum.

   2174. karumpukkaṭṭikku eṟumputāṉē varum.

   2175. karumpu muṟittuk kaḻutaiyai aṭitta katai.

   2177. karumpu tiṉṉak kūliyā?
   What, a reward for eating sugar-cane?
   2178. karumpum vēmpu āyiṟṟē.
   Even sugar cane has become a margosa tree.
   2179. karumpukku uḻuta, puḻuti kāycciṉa pālukkuc carukkarai āmā ?
   Will earth pulverised by the plough for sugar-cane, answer for sugar to

   2180. karumpu kacakkiṟatu vāykkuṟṟam.
   If sugar-cane provebitter to the palate, it is the fault of the mouth.

   2182. karumpai virumpa, atu vēmpu āyiṟṟu.
   If taken to excess sugar-cane becomes bitter as margosa.
   2183. karumpu kaṭṭōṭē irukka eṟumpu tāṉē varum.

   2184. karuvu illāta muṭṭaiyum kuru illāta vittaiyum.

   2185. karuvēlamarattiṟku niḻal illai, kaṉṉāṉukku muṟai illai.

   2186. karai kāṇāta tōṇipōla.
   As a doney out of sight of land.
   The common name of a country vessel, some of which are of considerable
   size. They have no deck and are thatched with palm leaves
   2187. karai taṭṭiṉ kappal pōla.
   Like a stranded vessel.
   2188. karaippakkam pātai irukkak kappal ēṟiṉavaṉum, collāta tai

   Like the misery endured by him who embarked when there was a way by

   2189. karaippār karaittāṟ kallum karaiyum.
   Under the management of the skilful, even a stone may be dissolved.
   2190. kalakattilē puḷukātavaṉ narakattilē pōvāṉām.
   It is said that he who does not exaggerate when a tumult occurs will go
   to hell.
   2191. kalakattilē pōyum kālmāṭu talaimāṭā?
   In regard to a bed is the distinction of head and foot observed in a
   battle-field?
   2192. kalakam kalantāl ulakam kalaṅkum.
   In a general insurrection universal consternation prevails.
   2193. kalakam piṟantāl niyāyam piṟakkum.
   Popular agitation leads to justice.
   2194. kalakamē meyyāṉāṟ puḷukātavaṉ pāvam.
   If an insurrection exists, it is wrong not to make it known.
   2195. kalakkantai kaṭṭik kāṇap pōṉāl irukalak kantai kaṭṭi etirē
   vantāḷ.
   When I went in rags to see her, she appeared enveloped in double the
   quantity.
   2196. kalak kampu tiṉṟālum kāṭai kāṭṭilē.
   Though it may consume a kalam of grain, the quail lives in the jungle.
   2197. kalakkiṉum kaṭal cēṟākātu.
   Though never so troubled, the sea does not, become muddy.
   2198. kalattilē cōṟṟai iṭṭuk kaiyaip piṭittāṟpōla.
   Like holding the hand after putting rice on the plate.
   2199. kala nellukkuk kala umi irukkumā?
   Will a kalam of paddy yield a kalam of husks?
   2200. kalap paṇattaip pārkkilum oru kiḻap piṇam nallatu.
   The corpse of an old person is better than immense wealth.
   2201. kalap pāl kaṟakkalām tuḷip pāl mulaikku ēṟṟalāmā ?
   A kalam of milk may be drawn; can a drop beinfused into the teat?
   2202. kalap pālukkut tuḷip purai.
   A drop of butter-milk or curds to a kalam of milk.
   2203. kalappāṉālum pūcapūcap poṉṉiṟam.
   Though not pure, repeated gilding will give it the colour of gold.
   2204. kalap pālil oru tuḷi viṣam kalantāṟpōlē.
   Like putting a drop of poisonin a kalam of milk.
   2205.kalap pāl kuṭittapūṉai āḻākkup pāl kuṭiyātā?
   Will not a cat that has drunk a kalam of milk drink alak?
   2206.kala mā iṭittavaḷ pāviyum, kappi iṭittavaḷ puṇṇiyavatiyumā?
   Is she who pounded a kalam of flour vicious, and she who pounded
   prolong(?) virtuous?
   2207. kalam kalantāl kulam kalakkum.
   When plates are interchanged, tribal marriages interchange.
   2208. kalam kuttiṉālum patar arici ākātu.
   Though a kalam of chaff bepounded, it will not become rice.
   2209. kala umi tiṉṟāl oru arici taṭṭātā?
   If a kalam of chaff be eaten may not a grain of rice turn up?
   2210. kaliyāṇac cantaṭiyil tāli kaṭṭa maṟantāṉām.
   It is said that he forgot to tie the tali owing to the bustle at the
   wedding.
   2211. kaliyāṇattilum pañcam illai, kaḷattilum pañcam illai.
   No scarcity at a wedding, nor in the threshing floor.
   2212. kaliyāṇa vīṭṭil pantaṟkālaik kaṭṭi aḻukiṟavaḷ cetta vīṭṭil cummā
   iruppāḷā?
   Will the woman who wept as sheclung to the post of a marriage pandal at
   be silent when she attends a funeral?
   2213. kaliyāṇa vīṭṭiṟkup pōy aṟiyāṉ, mēḷac cattamum kēṭṭu aṟiyāṉ.
   He has never been at a wedding, nor has he heard the sound of a
   tom-tom.
   2214. kaliyāṇam ceytum caṉṉiyāciyā?
   What, though married yet a Sanyasi-ascetic?
   2215. kal ākilum kaṇavaṉ, pul ākilum puruṣaṉ.
   Though in heart hard as a stone, and worthless as a blade of grass, he
   is your husband.
   2216. kal eṟikkut tappiṉālum kaṇ eṟikkut tappakkūṭātu.
   Though one may escape the cast of a stone, he cannot escape theglance
   of an evil eye.
   The evil eye kaṇṇūṟu and the evil tongue nāvūṟu are fearedvery
   generally. To avert their influence as regardsfruits &c, in a garden,
   it is common to put up some object of attraction as a water-pot
   whitewashed, inverted on a stump, and dotted with black spots &c. In
   some enclosures grotesque images are seen.
   2217. kallaṭic cittaṉ pōkiṟa vaḻi, kāṭu mēṭu ellām taviṭu poṭi.
   All the rough and rugged ground before the stone-cutter will be reduced
   to powder.
   2218. kallāṭam paṭittavaṉōṭu mal āṭātē
   Do not contend with him who has studied Kalladam.
   Kalladam a work on the third part of grammar is said to be extremely
   dry.
   2219.kallātār celvattilum kaṟṟār vaṟumai nalam.
   The poverty of the learned is better than the wealth of theunlearned.
   2220. kallāta oruvaṉ kulanalam pēcutal nelluṭaṉ piṟanta patarākummē.
   The illiterate who boast of high rank may be compared to the dust mixed
   with paddy-unhusked rice.
   2221. kallār uṟavilum kaṟṟōr pakai nalam.
   The enmity of the learned is better than the friendship of the
   illiterate.
   2222. kallār uṟavakal kāmak kaṭal kaṭa.
   Keep at a distance from the unlettered; cross the sea of passion.
   2223. kallilum vaṉmai kaṉa mūṭar neñcam.
   The heart of a great fool is harder than a stone.
   2224. kallilē nār urikkiṟatu pōla.
   Like stripping fibre from a stone.
   2225. kallum tēṅkāyum pōlap pēcukiṟāṉ.
   He speaks as effectively as the hitting of a stone on a cocoanut.
   2226. kallum kāvēriyum uḷḷa maṭṭum vāḻka.
   May you live as long as the rocks and the Cauveri exist.
   The river Cauveri rises in the western ghauts and flows into the bay of
   Bengal. It is the source of the great fertility of Tanjore and the
   adjacent districts. According to the legend it originated in the
   following manner. In ancient times when the people of southern India
   suffered from drought, Ganesa became incarnate in the form of a crow.
   As such he visited the abode of an ascetic on one of the western hills.
   He there alighted up the waterpot of a hermit and upset it. The water
   thus spilt, by reason of the hermit's merit, he came a river : the
   current spelling would make the name the lake of the crow: the legend
   requires the word to be spelt kālēṟi crow
   mounting, whereas kāvēri lake of the crow is common.
   2227. kallu varukiṟa vicaiyaik kaṇṭāl pallaic cikkeṉa mūṭa vēṇṭum.
   On seeing the rapid approach of a stone press your teeth and shut your
   mouth.
   2228. kallai ākilum karaikkalām, mūrkkaṉ maṉataik karaikkal ākātu.
   Even stones may be dissolved, but the mind of a fool cannot.
   2229. kallaik kaṇṭāl nāyaik kāṇōm, nāyaik kaṇṭāl kallaik kāṇōm.
   On finding a stone we see no dog, on seeing a dog we find no stone.
   2230. kallaik kuttuvāṉēṉ kai nōkiṟatu eṉṟu aḻuvāṉēṉ?
   Why beat a stone and then weep, exclaiming it has pained my hand?
   2231. kallaip pōlak kaṇavaṉ irukkak kañcikku aḻuvāṉēṉ?
   Why weep for kanji while you have a husband as hard as a stone ?
   2232. kallaik kiḷḷik kai iḻantatu pōlām.
   Like losing the hand by pinching a stone.
   2233. kalvi illāṉ celvam kaṟpillāḷ aḻaku.
   Wealth without learning is like beauty without chastity.
   2234. kalvi eṉṟa payirukkuk kaṇṇīr eṉṟa maḻai vēṇṭum.
   Plants of learning flourish in the rain of tears.
   2235. kalviyum kulamum velvatu viṉavil.
   When inquiring what will prevail, one's learning and rank naturally
   occur.
   2236. kalvi uḷḷa iḷaiñaṉ kaṉa kiḻavaṉ.
   A well instructed youth is an old man.
   2237. kalviyaip paṅkupōṭa ēlātu.
   Learning cannot be allotted in portions-to persons.
   2238. kavaṇeṟi neṟiyil nillātē, kaṇṭavaṉ talaiyai uṭaikkum.
   Do not stand in the way of a stone from a sling, it may break the head
   of the beholder.
   2239. kavi koṇṭārkkuk kīrtti, ataic cevikoḷḷārkku avakīrtti.
   Praise to those who approve of a poem, and blame to those who will not
   listen to it.
   2240. kavikkoṇṭārkkum kīrtti kalaippārkkum kīrttiyā?
   Are those who accept a poem and those who reject it alike praise
   worthy?
   2241. kavintirāṇam kajēntirāṇam rājāvē niṟpayē vaki.
   Oking, manage a poet laureate and a superior elephant without fear.
   2242. kavaiyaippaṟṟik kaḻutaiyiṉ kālaip piṭi.
   To effect your object, if necessary, cling to the legs of an ass.
   2243. kaḻutai kāṇavēṇṭiṉāl kuṭṭiccuvarilē kāṇalām.
   If an ass be wanted, it may be found feeding near a ruinous wall.
   2244. kaḻutaik keṭṭāl kuṭṭiccuvar.
   If an ass be out of condition, it will be as useless as a ruinous wall.
   2245. kaḻutaikku ēṉ kaṭivāḷam?
   Why a bridle for an ass?
   2246. kaḻutaip puṇṇukkup puḻuti maruntu.
   Dust is medicine for the gores of an ass.
   2247. kaḻutaiyum kutiraiyum cari āmā ?
   Are an ass and a horse equal ?
   2248. kaḻutaiyum kutiraiyum piṇaittāṟpōla.
   Like yoking an ass and a horse together.
   2249. kaḻutai aṟiyumā kantappoṭi vācaṉai?
   Can an ass appreciate fragrant powder?
   2250. kaḻutaikkuc cēṇam kaṭṭiṉālum kutirai āmā ?
   Does an ass become a horse by being saddled?
   2251. kaḻutai tēyntu kaṭṭeṟumpu āyiṟṟu.
   The ass by wearing away has become an ant.
   2252. kaḻutaikku ēṉ kaliṉam, allatu kaṭivāḷam?
   Why a bit or a bridle for an ass?
   2253. kaḻutaiyaik kaṭṭi ōmam vaḷarttatu pōla.
   Like tying an ass and feeding a sacrificial fire.
   This proverb relates to the following story. A certain Maharaja had a
   barber who had evinced great skill in shaving his royal master when he
   was asleep. To reward him the King resolved to elevate him in the
   socialscale. The brahmans were called and ordered to make a brahman of
   the barber. They set to work, kindled a sacred
   fire and the barber was led round it, whilst the priests uttered the
   required incantations for accomplishing their purpose. The prime
   minister being of a somewhat facetious temperament determined to
   metamorphose an ass. As the proverb intimates he kindled a fire and led
   the animal round it. When the King
   saw him thus engaged he asked him what he was doing. The prime minister
   replied by stating that if the brahmans could make a brahmanof a barber
   he thought an ass might be made into a horse, and he was therefore
   engaged in the ceremony.
   2254. kaḻutaikku upatēcam kātilē coṉṉālum apayak kural aṉṟi aṅku oṉṟum
   illai.
   Though religious instruction be whispered in the ears of an ass nothing
   will come of it but the accustomed braying.
   2255. kaḻutaikku vāḻkkaippaṭṭu utaikku añcalāmā?
   Having married an ass, do you fear his kicking ?
   2256. kaḻutaimēl ēṟi eṉṉa, iṟaṅki eṉṉa ?
   What matters it whether you mount an ass, or alight from one ?
   2257. kaḻutaimēl ēṟiyum perumai illai, iṟaṅkiyum ciṟumai illai.
   Though you may ride on an ass, you gain no honour, nor are you
   disgraced by dismounting.
   2258. kaḻutai uḻutu vaṇṇāṉ kuṭi āṉāṉā?
   Did the washerman become a farmer by ploughing with an ass ?
   2259. kaḻutai uḻutu kuṟavaṉ kuṭi āṉāṉā?
   Has the forester become a farmer by ploughing with an ass?
   2260. kaḻutai mayir piṭuṅkiṉāl ilāpam eṉṉa naṣṭam eṉṉa ?
   What profit or loss will arise from plucking off the hair of an ass ?
   2261. kaḻutai mayir piṭuṅkit tēcam kaṭṭi āḷvāṉā?
   Can one reign as a king by selling ass-hair?
   2262. kaḻutaikkut teriyumā kastūri vācaṉai?
   Does an ass appreciate the odour of musk?
   2263.kaḻutaikkup paratēcam kuṭṭiccuvar.
   The foreign country of an ass is a ruinous wall.
   2264. kaḻutai tappiṉāl kuṭṭiccuvariṭattil irukkum.
   If an ass goes astray, it may be found near a ruinous wall.
   2265. kaḻutaip potiyil aiṅkala māṟāṭṭamā?
   Are there five kalams of fraud in the pack on an ass?
   2266. kaḻutai viṭṭai āṉālum kai nirampa vēṇṭum eṉkiṟāy.
   You say get a handful, although it be but ass dung.
   2267. kaḻuttilē karimaṇi illai peyar muttumālai.
   Her name is pearl necklace, yet on her neck she has not a black bead.
   2268. kaḻuvik kaḻuvip piṉṉum cēṟṟai mitikkiṟatā?
   What ! is it to tread in the mud every time you wash your feet?
   2269. kaḻuvukku ēṟṟa kōmuṭṭi.
   A komutti exactly fitted to the stake.
   2270. kaḷam cāṟukiṟavaḷai miraṭṭuvāṉām pōr piṭuṅkukiṟavaṉ.
   It is said that he who steals grain from the stack, will frighten away
   the women who
   may sweep the threshing floor.
   2271. kaḷaiyak kūṭātataik kaṇṭāl aṭi peyarntu appuṟam pō.
   If you cannot pluck up, pass by on the other side.
   2272. kaḷaiyak kūṭātataik kaṇṭiyāmal cakittukkoḷ.
   Do not fret yourself about that which cannot be avoided.
   2273. kaḷ viṟṟuk kalappaṇam campātippatai viṭak kaṟpūram viṟṟuk
   kāṟpaṇam campātippatu mēl.
   It is better to gain a quarter of a fanam by selling camphor than a
   kalam by selling toddy.
   2274. kaḷḷaṉum āki viḷakkum piṭikkiṟāṉ.
   He plays the thief and also holds the lamp.
   2275. kaḷḷa vicuvācam kaḻuttellām cepamālai.
   The neck of the hypocrite is covered with rosaries.
   2276. kaḷḷaṉaiyum veḷḷa aṇaiyum kaṭṭiviṭu.
   Secure a thief by tying, and a flood by damming it up.
   2277. kaḷḷap piḷḷaiyilum cellappiḷḷai uṇṭā?
   Are there any indulged ones among the children of kallars?
   2278. kaḷḷaṉai āru naḷḷār eṉṟum.
   No one will ever approve of a thief.
   2279. kaḷḷaṉukku ūrellām viḷḷāp pakai.
   The whole country owes implacable hatred to a thief.
   2280.kaḷḷa māṭu cantai ēṟātu.
   A vicious bullock will not be brought to market.
   2281. kaḷḷaṉai nampiṉālum kuḷḷaṉai nampalākātu.
   A rogue may be trusted, but not a dwarf.
   2282. kaḷḷaṉ piṉ pōṉālum kuḷḷaṉ piṉ pōkappaṭātu.
   One may follow a rogue without risk, but not a dwarf.
   2283. kaḷḷaṉ accam kāṭu koḷḷātu.
   The jungle is not large enough to contain the fear of robbers.
   2284. kaḷḷa māṭu tuḷḷum.
   An unwilling bullock is restless.
   2285. kaḷḷa maṉam tuḷḷum.
   A guilty mind is agitated.
   2286. kaḷḷaṉ uṟavu uṟavum alla, kācā viṟaku viṟakum alla.
   The friendship of a rogue is not friendship, nor is kasawood fuel.
   2287. kaḷḷaṉ putti tiruṭṭu mēlē.
   The mind of a rogue is set on thieving.
   2288.kaḷḷaṉ piḷḷaikkum kaḷḷap putti.
   Even the child of a thief is characterized by thievish propensites.
   2289. kaḷḷaṉ maṉaivi kaimpeṇ eṉṟum.
   The wife of a thief is at any moment liable to become a widow.
   2290. kaḷḷaṉ maṉaiyāḷaik kaḷavup poruḷaik kāṇum kuṟi kēṭkalāmā?
   Can the wife of a thief be consulted as an augur for the recovery of
   stolen property ?
   2291. kaḷḷaṉum veḷḷamum oṉṟu.
   A thief and a flood are alike.
   2292. kaḷḷaṉukkup pāti veḷḷaṉukkup pāti.
   One half to the rogue, and one half to the honest.
   2293. kaḷḷaṉum tōṭṭakkāraṉum oṉṟu.
   A gardener is another term for a thief.
   2294. kaḷḷaṉum tōṭṭakkāraṉum oṉṟu kūṭiṉāl viṭiyumaṭṭum tiruṭalām.
   If the thief and a gardener are united, one may steal till day-break.
   2295. kaḷḷaṉ koṇṭa māṭu ettuṟai pōyeṉṉa?
   What matters it to what ferry the bull may go that has been bought by a
   thief?
   2296. kaḷḷaṉukkuk kāṇpittavaṉ pakai.
   He who points out a thief is hated by him.
   2297. kaḷḷi peruttu eṉṉa? kāy uṇṭā niḻal uṇṭā?
   What if the kalli grow large ? Has it any fruit or any shade ?
   2298. kaḷḷikku nāṭu ellām kāṭu.
   The whole country is a wild fit for the growth of the kalli.
   2299. kaḷḷik kompukku veḷḷippūṇ kaṭṭiṉatupōla.
   Like attaching a silver ferrule to a kalli stick.
   2300. kaḷḷi eṉṉaṭi kal iḻaippatu? kātil iruppatu pittaḷai.
   Why you hypocrite, inlay with gems ? that which is in the ear is made
   of brass.
   2301. kaḷḷikkum veṭṭik kaṟṟāḻaikkum veṭṭukiṟatu.
   After cutting the kalli to cut the kattalai.
   2302. kaḷḷikkut taṇṇīr kaṇṇilē, nīlikkut taṇṇīr nimaiyilē.
   The tears of a thievish woman are in her eyes, those of an abandoned
   woman are in her eyebrows.
   2303. kaḷḷikku kala nīr kaṇṇilē.
   A thievish woman has a kalam of tears in her eyes.
   2304. kaḷḷikku vēli ēṉ, cuḷḷikkuk kōṭāli ēṉ?
   What avails a fence against a thievish woman, or an axe for cutting
   twigs?
   2305. kaḷḷum cūtum irukkiṟa iṭattil kūttik kaḷḷaṉum iruppāṉ.
   Where there is theft and gambling, there will also be a whoremonger.
   2306. kaḷḷaik kuṭittāl uḷḷataic colvāṉ.
   Should he drink toddy, he will tell the truth.
   2307. kaṟakkiṟatu uḻakkup pāl, utaikkiṟatu pallup paṟakka.
   Yielding an ulak of milk, and kicking out the teeth.
   2308. kaṟanta pālum eccil piṟanta piḷḷaiyum eccil.
   First drawn milk is unclean, and so is a first-born child.
   2309. kaṟanta pāl mulaikku ēṟumā?
   Will the milk that has been drawn, again enter the udder?
   2310. kaṟanta pālaik kāyccāmaṟ kuṭittāl kāca viyāti tāṉē eṭupaṭum.
   If fresh drawn milk unboiled be drunk, consumption may be cured.
   2311. kaṟuttatu ellām taṇṇīr veḷuttatu ellām pāl eṉkiṟāṉ.
   Whatever is black he calls water, and whatever is white he calls milk.
   2312. kaṟuppu veḷuppu ākātu, kacappu iṉippu ākātu.
   Black will not become white, nor bitter sweet.
   2313. kaṟuppu nāy veḷḷai nāy ākumā?
   Will a black dog become white?
   2314. kaṟumuṟukāntap paṭalam vācikkiṟār kavirāyar.
   The poet is reading the section on grumbling.
   2315. kaṟaiyāṉ puṟṟil aravam kuṭikoṇṭatu pōla.
   Like a snake that occupied an ant-hill.
   2316. kaṟaiyāṉ puṟṟup pāmpukku utavukiṟatu.
   An ant-hill is useful to a snake.
   2317. kaṟkaṇṭāṟ ceyta eṭṭikkaṉi kacakkumā ?
   Will etti fruit made of sugar-candy taste bitter ?
   2318. kaṟkaiyil kalvi kacappu, kaṟṟapiṉpu atuvē iṉippu.
   Learning when being acquired is bitter, but when possessed it is sweet.
   2319. kaṟpakataruvaic cārnta kākamum amutam uṇṇum.
   Even a crow that lives in the kalpaka tree feeds on ambrosia.
   2320. kaṟpittavaṉ kaṇṇaik koṭuttavaṉ.
   He who gave the eye is he who taught.
   2321. kaṟpittavaṉ kāppāṟṟuvāṉ.
   He who preordained will protect or guide.
   2322. kaṟpillā aḻaku vācaṉai illāp pū.
   Beauty without chastity, a flower without fragrance.
   2323. kaṟṟa iṭattilā vittaiyaik kāṭṭukiṟatu?
   To practise a trick on him who taught it ?
   2324. kaṟṟavaratu kōpam nīriṉ piḷavupōl māṟum.
   The anger of the learned passes away as water recovers its surface
   after being
   divided.
   2325. kaṟṟatu kaiyaḷavu kallātu ulakaḷavu.
   What he has learnt is a handful, and what he has still to acquire is
   wide as the world.
   2326. kaṟṟatu collāṉ maṟṟu eṉṉa ceyvāṉ?
   What other service will he do for you who would not tell you what he
   has learnt ?
   2327. kaṟṟavaṉukku vittai kāl nāḻi.
   To the artist his art is but a quarter of a nali.
   2328. kaṟṟa vittaiyaik kāyccik kuṭikkiṟavaṉ pōlē.
   Like one who boils and drinks his learning.
   2329. kaṟṟaṟi mōḻaiyāy irātē.
   Be not a learned fool.
   2330. kaṟṟavaṉum uṇpāṉ, peṟṟavaḷum uṇpāḷ.
   A learned man and a fruitful woman will not suffer from want of food.
   2331. kaṟṟāḻai nāṟṟamum pittaḷai vīccamum pōkātu.
   The ill odour of the karrali and the bad smell emitted from brass will
   not leave them.
   2332. kaṟṟāḻai ciṟuttālum āṉai aṭi vaikkātu.
   Though the karralai be small, the elephant will not tread upon it.
   2333. kaṟṟuk koṭutta pēccum kaṭṭik koṭutta cātamum ettaṉai nāḷ niṟkum ?
   How long will words put into one's mouth, and rice prepared for a
   journey, last?
   2334. kaṟṟuk kaṟṟup pēcātē.
   Do not speak in a studied way.
   2335. kaṟṟum kaṟṟaṟimōḻai.
   Though he has learnt, he continues a learned fool.
   2336. kaṟṟum kaṟṟaṟimōḻaiyā?
   What, to be a learned fool?
   2337. kaṟṟōr arumai kaṟṟōr aṟivar.
   The learned know the worth of the learned.
   2338. kaṉa ācai kaṉa naṣṭam.
   Excessive desire entails great loss.
   2339. kaṉa eli vaḷai eṭātu.
   When rata are numerous, they do not burrow.
   2340. kaṉaka māri poḻintatu pōlē.
   As it rained gold.
   2341. kaṉañ ceytāl iṣṭam, kaṉavīṉattāl naṣṭam.
   Dignity gains approval, meanness entails loss.
   2342. kaṉatta uṭamaikku aṉarttam illai.
   Solid jewels are invaluable.
   2343. kaṉattāl iṉamākum paṇattāl caṉamākum.
   Dignity or worth increases relatives, wealth secures society.
   2344. kaṉattiṟku naṟkuṇam cumaitāṅki.
   Virtue is the support of dignity.
   2345. kaṉattaik kaṉam aṟiyum karuvāṭṭup pāṉaiyai nāy aṟiyum.
   Dignity is appreciated by dignity, the pot of dried fish is known to
   the dog.
   2346. kaṉa mūṭaṉ kaipporuḷ iḻappāṉ.
   A great fool loses his substance.
   2347. kaṉavilum kākkaikku malam tiṉkiṟatē niṉaivu.
   Even in its dreams, the crow's thoughts turn on eating filth.
   2348. kaṉavil kaṇṭa paṇam celaviṟku utavumā?
   Will the money seen in a dream meet one's expenses?
   2349. kaṉavil kaṇṭa poruḷ kāṉalil kaṇṭa puṉal.
   The wealth seen in a dream, the water observed in a mirage.
   2350. kaṉavil uṇṭa cōṟu paci tīrkkumā?
   Will the rice consumed in a dream appease hunger ?
   2351. kaṉavilē kaṇṭavaṉukkup peṇ koṭutta katai.
   The story of a woman given in marriage to a man seen in a dream.
   2352. kaṉinta paḻam tāṉē viḻum.
   Ripe fruit falls of itself.
   2353.kaṉinta paḻam nīr tiṉṟīr kāyai ulukkiviṭṭīr.
   You have eaten the ripe fruit, and you have shaken off the unripe.
   2354. kaṉiyai viṭṭuk kāyait tiṉkiṟatā?
   What, to leave ripe fruit and to eat unripe ?
   2355. kaṉmattiṉāṟ cātiyaṉṟic ceṉmattiṉāl illai.
   Caste arises from action, it is not from birth.
   2356. kaṉamaḻai peytālum karuṅkal karaiyumā ?
   Will granite be dissolved by any amount of hail?
   2357. kaṉmattiṉāl vantatu taṉ mantiṉāl pōkavēṇṭum.
   That which has come of action sin must go by alms giving
   religiousmerit.
   2358. kaṉṟu irukkak kācattaṉai pāl kaṟavāp pacu kaṉṟu cetta piṟaku
   kalappāl kaṟakkumā?
   Will a cow that gave milk worth a small coin while its calf lived, give
   a kalam after her calf is dead ?
   2359. kaṉṟukkuṭṭiyai aviḻkkac coṉṉārkaḷā kaṭṭuttaṟiyaip piṭuṅkac
   coṉṉārkaḷā?
   Did they tell you to loose the calf or to pull up the peg?
   2360. kaṉṟum pacuvum kāṭēṟi mēyntāl kaṉṟu kaṉṟu vaḻiyē pacu pacu
   vaḻiyē.
   When the calf and cow go out to graze, each takes its own way.
   2361. kaṉṟukaḷāyk kūṭik kaḷai paṟikkap pōṉāl vaikkōl ākumā cettai
   ākumā?
   If a herd of calves weed the corn, will there be any straw, or ever dry
   stubble?
   2362. kaṉṟaik kaṇṭu ōṭivarum pacuvaippōlē.
   Like a cow that comes running on seeing her calf.
   2363. kaṉṉāṉ naṭamāṭak kuyavaṉ kuṭipōvāṉ.
   When the brazier begins to move, the potter will abandon his
   dwelling-place.
   2364. kaṉṉāṉukku muṉṉē kaḻutai paratēcam pōṉāṟpōlē.
   As an ass went on a pilgrimage before a copper-smith.
   2365. kaṉṉi irukkak kāḷai maṇam pōkalāmā?
   When a virgin is yet unmarried, may the youth-her brother marry?
   2366. kākattilē veḷḷai uṇṭā?
   Are there any white crows ?
   2367. kākattiṉ kaṇṇukkup pīrkkampū poṉ niṟam.
   To the eye of a crow the flower of the gourd is tinged with gold.
   2368. kākam kar eṉṟāl kaṇavaṉai appā eṉṟu kaṭṭikkoḷḷuvāḷām.
   It is said that when a crow caws she will exclaim, alas, and cling to
   her husband.
   2369. kākam ēṟip paṉaṅkāy utirumā ?
   Will palmyra fruit fall because a crow alights on the tree ?
   2370. kākam illāta ūr pāvi illāta ūr.
   Vicious persons are not found in a village which has no crows.
   2371. kākam irukkap paḻam viḻuntatupōla.
   Like the dropping of the fruit when a crow alighted on the tree.
   2372. kākam illāta ūr cōṉakaṉ illāta ūr.
   The country in which crows do not exist is without mussalmans.
   2373. kākam kālamē kā kā eṉṟu kattum.
   Crows will caw at day-break.
   2374. kākkaṉukkum pūkkaṉukkum pūttāyō puṉṉai kaṇṇāḷar varuntaṉaiyum
   poṟukkalaiyō puṉṉai?
   O punnai tree, Alexandrian laurel, hast thou blossomed for Kakan and
   Pookan, couldst not thou have waited till the arrival of my husband ?
   2375. kākkāyk kuñcu āṉālum taṉ kuñcu poṉ kuñcu.
   Though but a young crow, it is a golden one to its mother.
   2376. kākkaiyiṟ karitu kaḷāmpaḻam.
   Kala fruit, earissa, is blacker than a crow.
   2377. kākkaiyum kuyiṟ kuñcait taṉ kuñcu pōl vaḷarkkum.
   Even a crow will bring up a young kuyil-India cuckoo-as her own.
   2378. kākkaikku añcu kuṇam.
   The crow has five characteristics.
   2379. kākkai ēṟiṉatum paṉampaḻam viḻuntatum.
   The alighting of the crow, and the falling of the palmyra fruit.
   2380. kākkaiyum kattik kattip pōkiṟatu karuvāṭum ularntu pōkiṟatu.
   The crowcontinues to caw whilst the karuvadu,(salted fish) dries.
   2381. kākkaiyum kāṟṟum pōkku uṇṭāṉāl varum.
   Crows and windsgo but to return.
   2382. kākkaiyaik kaṇṭu añcuvāḷ karaṭiyaip piṭittuk kaṭṭuvāḷ.
   She is afraid on seeing a crow, and yet she will capture a bear.
   2383.kākkai ēṟiṉ kompu acaiyātā?
   Will not the branch shake on which a crow alights?
   2384. kāci iraṇṭu eḻuttuttāṉ kāṇa ettaṉai nāṭcellum?
   Kasi is formed of two letters only; how many days will it take to reach
   it?
   2385. kācikkup pōyum karumam tolaiyavillai.
   Though one has gone to Benares his sins are not removed.
   2386. kācikkup pōṉālum akappai araikkācu.
   Though taken to Benares, awooden ladle will fetch but half acash.
   2387. kācikkup pōṉālum kati peṟa vaḻi illai.
   Although he may go to Kasi, he will not find the way to heaven.
   2388. kācikkuppōyum muṭattavaci kālil viḻukiṟatā?
   What ! having gone to Kasi is it to fall at the feet of a lame ascetic?
   2389. kāciyil irukkiṟavaṉ kaṇṇaik kuttak kāñcipurattiliruntu kaiyai
   nīṭṭikkoṇṭu pōkiṟatā?
   If you wish to strike the eyes of one at Kasi, do you go, for that
   purpose, with outstretched band from Conjevaram?
   2390. kācu illātavaṉukku varākaṉ pēccu eṉṉa?
   Why should a penniless man talk about pagodas?
   2391. kācu koṭuttāl vēci varuvāḷ, kala nellaik koṭuttāl avaḷ akkāḷum
   āttāḷum kūṭa varuvārkaḷ.
   If you are free with your money a harlot willcome, if you give also a
   kalam of rice, har sister and her mother will come.
   2392. kācukku oru kuñcu viṟṟālum kaṇakkak kuñcu ākātu.
   Though young birds are sold a cash each, an accountant's young bird is
   not good.
   2393. kācukku oru kuñcu viṟṟālum kammāḷak kuñcu ākātu.
   Though sold for a single cash, never buya smith's bird.
   2394. kācukku oru puṭaivai viṟṟālum nāyiṉ cūttu ammaṇam.
   Though cloth may be cheap; dogs go naked.
   2395. kācukkūṭu karikkūṭāyp pōyiṟṟu.
   The money basket has become a charcoal basket.
   2396. kācukkup pōṉa māṉam kōṭi koṭuttālum varātu.
   Chastity lost for a cash will not be recovered by a crore.
   2397. kācaik koṭuttuk kuttu māṭu tēṭukiṟatā?
   Will you buy a vicious cow at a high price?
   2398.kāñcipurattiṟkup pōṉāl kāl āṭṭi uṇṇalām.
   If you go to Conjeverarm, you may eat without labour.
   2399. kāṭikkup pōyt tayir koṇṭuvantatu pōla.
   Like going for vinegar and bringing curds.
   2400. kāṭu kāttavaṉum kaṟcēri kāttavaṉum palaṉ aṭaivāṉ.
   Both will be benefited, he who watches the jungle and he who waits at
   the cutchery.
   2401. kāṭu vā eṉkiṟatu vīṭu pō eṉkiṟatu.
   Home bids me go, and the place of incremation sayscome.
   2402. kāṭu viḷaintālum oru mēṭu viḷaintālum kaṭaṉ kaḻintupōm.
   The debt will be discharged in either case, whether the jungle or
   highland yield a
   good crop.
   2403. kāṭu viḷaiyāviṭṭālum kaṭamai pōkumā?
   Though the waste land has yielded nothing, will the tax be remitted?
   2404. kāṭu ventāṟ cantaṉa maramum vēkātō?
   Should the jungle be consumed, would the sandal wood tree escape?
   2405. kāṭai iṭam āṉāl nāṭṭai āḷalām.
   If a quail crosses one's path to the left, he will govern a province.
   2406. kāṭṭik koṭuttuk kaṭakkap pōy niṟkalāmā?
   Is it proper to betray one, and then stand aloof?
   2407. kāṭṭil āṉaiyaik kāṭṭi vīṭṭil peṇṇaik koṭukkiṟatā?
   Is it to give a girl in marriage when an elephant in the jungle is
   shown as dower?
   2408. kāṭṭilē cettālum vīṭṭilētāṉ tīṭṭu.
   Though a man dies in the jungle, ceremonial uncleanness will attach to
   his house.
   2409. kāṭṭup pūṉaikkuc civarāttiri viratamā?
   Does the wild cat observe the fast of Sivaratri?
   2410. kāṭṭukku eṟitta nilāvum kāṉalukkup peyta maḻaiyum.
   Moonlight in the jungle and rain in the plains.
   2411. kāṭṭu vāḻai vantāl vīṭṭu vāḻvu pōkum.
   When wild plantains come, domestic prosperity will vanish.
   2412. kāṭṭup pēyccuraikkāy kaṟikku ākumā?
   Is a wild gourd fit for curry?
   2413. kāṭṭuk kōḻikku uraṟ kuḻiyē kailācam.
   The hole in a mortar is paradise to a jungle fowl.
   2414. kāṭṭup pūccikaḷ etirtta katai āccu.
   It has become the story of wild insects offering resistance.
   2415. kāṇak kiṭaittatu kārttikaip piṟaipōla.
   That which was seen was like the new moon of November.
   2416. kāṇappaṭṭaṉa ellām aḻiyappaṭṭaṉa.
   All that is seen is temporary.
   2417. kāṇāmal kaṇṭēṉō kampaṅkatirē cintāmal kuttaṭi cilli mūkkē ?
   Have I seen kambu-millet for the first time, pound it without
   scattering thou nose
   bleeding woman?
   2418. kāṇi āḷaṉ vīṭu vēkaccē kālaip piṭittu iḻutta katai.
   The story of one pulling by the feet the landlord whose house was on
   fire.
   2419. kāṇi ēṟak kōṭi aḻiyum.
   While 1/80 is added a crore is destroyed.
   2420. kāṇi kaviḻntu pōkiṟatā ?
   Does land turn upside down?
   2421. kāṇi kāṇiyāyc campātittuk kōṭi kōṭiyāyc celavaḻikka vēṇṭum.
   We must acquire by eightieths and spend by crores.
   2422. kāṇikku ottatu kōṭikku.
   The profit or loss on an eightieth part of a unit, will determine that
   on a crore.
   2423. kāṇi cōmpal kōṭi varuttam.
   A little indolence creates great trouble.
   2424. kāṇi mantam kōṭi tukkam.
   A little indolence will bring great sorrow
   2425. kātam viṭṭu irukātam cuṟṟukiṟatu pōla.
   Like ranging over the distance of two kathams instead of one.
   2426. kātam koṭuttu irukkātam vāṅkukiṟatu pōla.
   Like giving a katham and getting two.
   2427. kātavaḻi pōy aṟiyātāṉ mātam ellām naṭantāṉām.
   He who never walked a katham is said to have walked a whole month.
   2428. kātavaḻi pōy aṟiyāṉ kaḻutaip piṟappu.
   He who never walked a katham is an ass.
   2429. kātavaḻi peyar illāṉ kaḻutaiyōṭu okkum.
   He whose reputation does not reach a katham is like an ass.
   2430. kātilē nārācam ēṟṟiṉāṟ pōla.
   Like an iron wire passed into the ear.
   2431. kātil cilanti ōtaṭi āṉanti.
   O Ananti, I have a boil in my ear, utter an incantation.
   2432. kātu aṟutta kūli kaimēlē.
   The hire for tearing the ear is readily paid.
   2433. kātu aṟuttālum aṟukkum pēṉaip pārttālum pārkkum kuraṅku.
   A monkey may tear the ears or hunt lice if he choose.
   2434. kātu kātu eṉṟāl nāti nāti eṉkiṟāṉ.
   If one says, my ear, my ear, he replies it is mine, it is mine.
   2435. kātu kātu eṉṟāl ceviṭu ceviṭu eṉkiṟāṉ.
   If one cry an ear, an ear, he cries deafness deafness.
   2436. kātu kuttap poṟukkātā?
   Can you not bear the boring of your ears?
   2437. kātukku iṭṭāl mukattukku aḻaku.
   If worn in the ears, they will add beauty to the face.
   2438. kātukkuk kaṭukkaṉ iṭṭu āṭṭikkoṇṭu tirikiṟāṉ.
   Having put on ear-rings he walks about shaking them.
   2439. kātum kātum vaittāṟpōl irukkavēṇṭum.
   It should be as if an ear, and an ear, were brought in contact.
   2440. kātai aṟuttavaṉ kaṇṇaiyum kuttuvāṉā?
   Will he who cut off the ear strike the eye also ?
   2441. kāttiruntavaṉ peṇṭāṭṭiyai vēṟṟu maṉitaṉ piṭittukkoṇṭāṉ.
   A stranger took possession of the wife of him who was watching her.
   2442. kāntamum ūciyum pōl.
   Like a magnet and needle.
   2443. kāppāṉukkuk kaḷḷaṉ illai.
   He who takes care of his property will not be robbed.
   2444. kāyam paṭṭa kuraṅkupōl.
   Like a wounded monkey.
   2445. kāyum puḻuviṟkuc cāyum niḻal pōla.
   Like a falling shadow on a sun-striken worm.
   2446. kāytta marattil kal eṟipaṭum kāyāta marattil kal eṟi paṭumā?
   Stones are thrown at a fruit bearing tree; are they thrown at that
   which does not
   bear?
   2447. kāytta marattilē kal eṟiyum cil eṟiyum.
   Stones and other missiles are thrown at a fruit-bearing tree.
   2448. kāytta maram vaḷaintu niṟkum naṟkuṇam uṭaiyavar taṇintu niṟpār.
   A fruit-bearing tree bends; the virtuous are lowly.
   2449. kāynta maram taḷirkkumā ?
   Will a dry tree bud?
   2450. kāynta māṭu kampilē viḻuntāṟpōla.
   Like a starving cow falling in the kambu-millet.
   2451. kāynta puli āṭṭu mantaiyilē viḻukiṟatupōla .
   Like a hungry tiger falling on a flock of sheep.
   2452. kāynta puli āvilē viḻukiṟatu.
   A lean tiger falls on a cow.
